> اللهَ اللهَ يَا مُعَاوِيَةُ فِي أُمَّةِ مُحَمَّدٍ -صَلَّى اللَّهُ عَلَيْهِ وَسَلَّمَ-! هَبُوْا أَنَّكُمْ قَتَلْتُم أَهْلَ العِرَاقِ، فَمَنْ لِلْبُعُوْثِ وَالذَّرَارِي؟ أَمْ هَبُوْا أَنَّا قَتَلْنَاكُم، فَمَنْ لِلْبُعُوْثِ وَالذَّرَارِي؟ إِنَّ اللهَ يَقُوْلُ: {وَإِنْ طَائِفَتَانِ مِنَ المُؤْمِنِيْنَ اقْتَتَلُوا، فَأَصْلِحُوا بَيْنَهُمَا}
> — ⁨سیر أعلام النبلاء ج۲ ص۴۱⁩ · https://shamela.ws/book/10906/2021
> *English:* "God, God, O ⁨معاوية⁩, in the matter of the Umma of Muḥammad ﷺ! Suppose you kill the
> people of Iraq: who is left for the frontier expeditions and the children? Or suppose we kill you:
> who is left for the frontier expeditions and the children? God says: *and if two parties of the
> believers fight, then make peace between them.*"

⁨معاوية ؓ⁩ said: *what do you want?* He said: *let us at the water.* And ⁨معاوية ؓ⁩ said to ⁨أبو الأعور⁩:
*let our brothers at the water* `[SOURCED]` ⁨سیر ج۲ ص۴۱⁩. The editor traces it through
⁨عبد الله بن الإمام أحمد⁩'s book **⁨صفين⁩**, from ⁨أحمد⁩, from ⁨أبو المغيرة⁩ `[SOURCED]` ⁨سیر ج۲ ص۴۱⁩,
footnote (١). **This is the best single card in the whole strand**: two Companions, on opposite sides
of a river, settling one thing by the Qurʾān — and it lets the speaker say "they were not what we
imagine" without saying one word of judgement.

**A second scene worth telling.** ⁨أبو إسحاق⁩ prayed ⁨ف⁩ajr in ⁨الأشعث⁩'s mosque and found a purse and a
pair of sandals set before every single man in the congregation — ⁨الأشعث⁩ had come in the night and
said: *look — every man who prays the morning prayer in our mosque, set a purse and a pair of
sandals before him.* Another route has it as a robe and sandals `[SOURCED]` ⁨سیر ج۲ ص۴۱⁩–۴۲. And
⁨ميمون بن مهران⁩: **he was the first man beside whom men walked on foot while he rode** `[SOURCED]` ⁨سیر⁩
ج۲ ص۴۲.

**The date and manner of death.** **40 AH**, and some add: **forty nights after ⁨علي ؓ⁩**; he was buried
in his own house; it is said he lived sixty-three years. **⁨الحسن بن علي ؓ⁩** came to them, told them to
wash him with camphor, and — per ⁨محمد بن سعد⁩ — **prayed over him**; he died at ⁨الكوفة⁩ while ⁨الحسن ؓ⁩
was there, at the time he made peace with ⁨معاوية ؓ⁩ `[SOURCED]` ⁨سیر ج۲ ص۴۲⁩.

**⚠ What not to say about him from the platform.** The report that at ⁨التحكيم⁩ he insisted
`⁨لاَ يَكُوْنُ فِيْهَا مُضَرِيَّانِ أَبَداً، حَتَّى يَكُوْنَ أَحَدُهُمَا يَمَانٍ⁩` — that no two
Muḍarīs would arbitrate and one must be a Yemeni — is in ⁨سیر ج۲ ص۳۹۵⁩, **but its chain runs through
⁨الواقدي⁩, whom the editor marks ⁨متروك⁩**, and the parallel report on the same page is also through
⁨الواقدي⁩ `[SOURCED]` ⁨سیر ج۲ ص۳۹۵⁩ and its footnotes (٣) and (٤). It is the standard explanation of why
⁨أبو موسى ؓ⁩ was chosen. **Do not put it on a slide as fact.** If the tribal dimension must be named,
name it as *what ⁨ابن سعد⁩ reports through ⁨الواقدي⁩*, and say plainly that the chain is weak.

---

## 11. حذيفة بن اليمان ؓ

**The ⁨تراجم⁩ notice, in one breath**

- `name + ⁨لقب⁩` — **⁨حذيفة بن اليمان بن جابر العبسي ؓ⁩**, ⁨أبو عبد الله⁩. ⁨الذهبي⁩ heads him
  **⁨مِنْ نُجَبَاءِ أَصْحَابِ مُحَمَّدٍ ﷺ⁩**, and gives him the ⁨لقب⁩ the tradition itself gave him:
  **⁨صاحب السِّرّ⁩** — the keeper of the secret, that is, of the names of the hypocrites
  `[SOURCED]` ⁨سیر ج۲ ص۳۶۱⁩ and its footnote (١).
- `dates` — at **⁨أحد⁩** (3 AH), where his father was killed; **died at ⁨المدائن⁩ in 36 AH, forty nights
  after ⁨عثمان ؓ⁩**, an old man `[SOURCED]` ⁨سیر ج۲ ص۳۶۲⁩ · ⁨ج۲ ص۳۶۴⁩ · ⁨ج۲ ص۳۶۶⁩. On the Line: he dies
  **before ⁨الجمل⁩**, and that is the fact the whole session turns on.
- `what he did` — he was the Prophet's ﷺ scout on the night of **⁨الأحزاب⁩**, the man entrusted with the
  names of the hypocrites, the conqueror of **⁨الدينور⁩**, and **⁨عمر ؓ⁩'s governor of ⁨المدائن⁩** — which
  he was still governing when ⁨عثمان ؓ⁩ was killed `[SOURCED]` ⁨سیر ج۲ ص۳۶۳⁩–۳۶۴.

**Clan and kin.** **⁨عبس⁩**, of the north — but his family lived in Medina as **⁨حلفاء⁩ of the Anṣār**,
of **⁨بني عبد الأشهل⁩**, and ⁨الذهبي⁩ also counts him `⁨مِنْ أَعْيَانِ المُهَاجِرِيْنَ⁩` `[SOURCED]` ⁨سیر⁩
⁨ج۲ ص۳۶۱⁩. His father's name was **⁨حِسْل⁩** (or ⁨حُسَيل⁩); he had killed a man among his own people and
fled to Medina, and because he allied with the Yemenis — that is, the Anṣār — his people called him
**⁨اليمان⁩** `[SOURCED]` ⁨سیر ج۲ ص۳۶۲⁩. His brother **⁨صفوان بن اليمان⁩** was at ⁨أحد⁩ `[SOURCED]` ⁨سیر⁩
⁨ج۲ ص۳۶۹⁩. ⁨الواقدي⁩ and ⁨ابن إسحاق⁩ both record that the Prophet ﷺ made him and **⁨عمار بن ياسر ؓ⁩**
brothers `[SOURCED]` ⁨سیر ج۲ ص۳۶۲⁩.

**Before 23 AH.** Three things the room must have. **(a)** At **⁨أحد⁩** his father was killed **by
Muslims, by mistake** — ⁨حذيفة ؓ⁩ was shouting *my father! my father!* and they did not hear; and he
said *may God forgive you* and **gave his father's blood-money away as ṣadaqa to the Muslims**
`[SOURCED]` ⁨سیر ج۲ ص۳۶۲⁩ and its footnote (٢), which cites ⁨البخاري ۷⁩/۲۹۷. The words themselves stand
in the editor's footnote, unvocalised, not in ⁨الذهبي⁩'s own text — so they are given here in English
only; the Arabic of the main text is the statement quoted in **E-PG49**. **(b)** He was not at **⁨بدر⁩**
— he and his father had been stopped by Quraysh on the road and made to swear they would not fight;
the Prophet ﷺ said: `⁨نَفِيْ بِعَهْدِهِمْ، وَنَسْتَعِيْنُ اللهَ عَلَيْهِم⁩` — *we keep their covenant
and we ask God's help against them* (⁨مسلم ۱۷۷۸⁩) `[SOURCED]` ⁨سیر ج۲ ص۳۶۴⁩. **(c)** He was the man sent
out alone on the night of **⁨الأحزاب⁩** to bring back news of the enemy (⁨مسلم ۱۷۸۸⁩) `[SOURCED]` ⁨سیر⁩
ج۲ ص۳۶۴.

**In this window.** ⁨عمر ؓ⁩ appointed him over **⁨المدائن⁩** and wrote in his commission: *hear him,
obey him, and give him whatever he asks you for* `[SOURCED]` ⁨سیر ج۲ ص۳۶۳⁩. He left ⁨عمر ؓ⁩ on a saddled
donkey with his provisions under him, and the Persian district chiefs came out to receive him — and
he was holding a loaf and a bone with meat on it `[SOURCED]` ⁨سیر ج۲ ص۳۶۳⁩ · ⁨ج۲ ص۳۶۶⁩. Asked what he
wanted from them, he said: **food that I can eat, and fodder for this donkey of mine, of straw, as
long as I am among you** `[SOURCED]` ⁨سیر ج۲ ص۳۶۶⁩. ⁨الدينور⁩ was opened by force at his hands
`[SOURCED]` ⁨سیر ج۲ ص۳۶۴⁩. **He held ⁨المدائن⁩ until after the killing of ⁨عثمان ؓ⁩** `[SOURCED]` ⁨سیر⁩
ج۲ ص۳۶۴.

**One ⁨واقعہ⁩ worth telling — ⁨عمر ؓ⁩ lies in wait on the road.** ⁨ابن سيرين⁩: ⁨عمر ؓ⁩ sent ⁨حذيفة ؓ⁩ to
⁨المدائن⁩, and when he had been there as long as God willed, ⁨عمر ؓ⁩ wrote to him: *come.* And when ⁨عمر ؓ⁩
heard that he was coming, **he hid himself on the road** — to see with his own eyes what governorship
had done to the man. And when he saw him arriving in exactly the state in which he had left, ⁨عمر ؓ⁩
came out to him, took hold of him, and said:

> أَنْتَ أَخِي، وَأَنَا أَخُوْكَ.
> — ⁨سیر أعلام النبلاء ج۲ ص۳۶۶⁩ · https://shamela.ws/book/10906/2346
> *English:* "You are my brother, and I am yours."

`[SOURCED]` ⁨سیر ج۲ ص۳۶۶⁩.

**His own words.** These are the sentences the whole of session 3 can be built on.

> كَانَ النَّاسُ يَسْأَلُوْنَ رَسُوْلَ اللهِ -صَلَّى اللَّهُ عَلَيْهِ وَسَلَّمَ- عَنِ الخَيْرِ، وَكُنْتُ أَسْأَلُهُ عَنِ الشَّرِّ، مَخَافَةَ أَنْ يُدْرِكَنِي.
> — ⁨سیر أعلام النبلاء ج۲ ص۳۶۵⁩ · https://shamela.ws/book/10906/2345
> *English:* "People used to ask the Messenger of God ﷺ about the good; and I used to ask him about
> the evil, for fear that it would overtake me."

⁨البخاري⁩, in ⁨علامات النبوة⁩; the editor gives the whole ḥadīth in the footnote, ending
`⁨فاعتزل تلك الفرق كلها⁩` `[SOURCED]` ⁨سیر ج۲ ص۳۶۵⁩ and footnote (٣).

> وَاللهِ إِنِّي لأَعْلَمُ النَّاسِ بِكُلِّ فِتْنَةٍ هِيَ كَائِنَةٌ فِيْمَا بَيْنِي وَبَيْنَ السَّاعَةِ
> — ⁨سیر أعلام النبلاء ج۲ ص۳۶۵⁩ · https://shamela.ws/book/10906/2345
> *English:* "By God, I am the most knowledgeable of people about every fitna that will occur between
> now and the Hour." (⁨مسلم ۲۸۹۱⁩.)

> مَا النِّفَاقُ؟ قَالَ: أَنْ تَتَكَلَّمَ بِالإِسْلاَمِ، وَلاَ تَعْمَلَ بِهِ.
> — ⁨سیر أعلام النبلاء ج۲ ص۳۶۳⁩ · https://shamela.ws/book/10906/2343
> *English:* "'What is hypocrisy?' He said: 'That you speak Islam and do not act by it.'"

> إِنِّي لأَشْتَرِي دِيْنِي بَعْضُهُ بِبَعْضٍ، مَخَافَةَ أَنْ يَذْهَبَ كُلُّهُ.
> — ⁨سیر أعلام النبلاء ج۲ ص۳۶۸⁩ · https://shamela.ws/book/10906/2348
> *English:* "I buy part of my religion with part of it, for fear that all of it should go."

And **⁨علي ؓ⁩**, asked about him:

> عَلِمَ المُنَافِقِيْنَ، وَسَأَلَ عَنِ المُعْضِلاَتِ؛ فَإِنْ تَسْأَلُوْهُ، تَجِدُوْهُ بِهَا عَالِماً.
> — ⁨سیر أعلام النبلاء ج۲ ص۳۶۳⁩ · https://shamela.ws/book/10906/2343
> *English:* "He knew the hypocrites, and he asked about the hard cases; if you ask him, you will
> find him knowing them." (Editor: `⁨رجاله ثقات⁩`.)

**The date and manner of death, and the sentence that ends the session.** He died **at ⁨المدائن⁩, in
36 AH**, **forty nights after ⁨عثمان ؓ⁩** `[SOURCED]` ⁨سیر ج۲ ص۳۶۴⁩ · ⁨ج۲ ص۳۶۶⁩. ⁨الحسن البصري⁩ records what
he said as death came:

> حَبِيْبٌ جَاءَ عَلَى فَاقَةٍ؛ لاَ أَفْلَحَ مَنْ نَدِمَ! أَلَيْسَ بَعْدِي مَا أَعْلَمُ! الحَمْدُ للهِ الَّذِي سَبَقَ بِي الفِتْنَةَ! قَادَتَهَا وَعُلُوْجَهَا.
> — ⁨سیر أعلام النبلاء ج۲ ص۳۶۸⁩ · https://shamela.ws/book/10906/2348
> *English:* "A beloved one has come at a time of need. He does not prosper who regrets! Is there not
> after me what I know of? Praise be to God, who has taken me on ahead of the fitna — its leaders and
> its brutes."

`[SOURCED]` ⁨سیر ج۲ ص۳۶۸⁩. ⁨النزال بن سبرة⁩ asked ⁨أبو مسعود الأنصاري ؓ⁩ what ⁨حذيفة ؓ⁩ said at his death, and
he said: at the last watch of the night he said three times *I seek refuge in God from a morning that
leads to the Fire*, and then: **buy me two white garments, for they will not stay on me long before I
am given better than them in exchange, or stripped of them in an ugly stripping** `[SOURCED]` ⁨سیر⁩
⁨ج۲ ص۳۶۸⁩. When a robe worth three hundred was brought, he refused it: *no — buy me two white
garments* `[SOURCED]` ⁨سیر ج۲ ص۳۶۹⁩. And ⁨جُزَيّ بن بكير⁩: `⁨لَمَّا قُتِلَ عُثْمَانُ، فَزِعْنَا إِلَى⁩
⁨حُذَيْفَةَ، فَدَخَلْنَا عَلَيْهِ⁩` — *when ⁨عثمان⁩ was killed, we fled in alarm to ⁨حذيفة⁩, and went in to
him* `[SOURCED]` ⁨سیر ج۲ ص۳۶۹⁩. **That is the moment: the man who had been told what was coming was the
man they ran to when it came, and he had forty nights left.**

**⚠ What not to say about him from the platform.** Do not use the report that ⁨حذيفة ؓ⁩ said of two
Companions entering the mosque that one of them was a hypocrite — see **Not for the hall**, where
both ⁨الذهبي⁩'s own words and the editor's are recorded. Do not use the two reports about a gold ring
with two cranes on it: the editor notes that **⁨أم موسى⁩, the sole narrator, is unknown**, and that the
prohibition on gold for men is firmly established `[SOURCED]` ⁨سیر ج۲ ص۳۶۷⁩ and its footnotes.

---

## 12. قيس بن سعد بن عبادة ؓ

**The ⁨تراجم⁩ notice, in one breath**

- `name + ⁨لقب⁩` — **⁨قيس بن سعد بن عبادة الأنصاري الخزرجي الساعدي ؓ⁩**, ⁨أبو عبد الله⁩. ⁨الذهبي⁩ heads him
  **⁨الأَمِيْرُ، المُجَاهِدُ⁩ ... ⁨سَيِّدُ الخَزْرَجِ وَابْنُ سَيِّدِهِم⁩**, and — the phrase to use —
  `⁨صَاحِبُ رَسُوْلِ اللهِ⁩ -⁨صَلَّى اللَّهُ عَلَيْهِ وَسَلَّمَ⁩ - ⁨وَابْنُ صَاحِبِهِ⁩` `[SOURCED]` ⁨سیر ج۳ ص۱۰۲⁩.
- `dates` — he says of himself `⁨صَحِبْتُ النَّبِيَّ⁩ -⁨صَلَّى اللَّهُ عَلَيْهِ وَسَلَّمَ⁩- ⁨عَشْرَ سِنِيْنَ⁩` — ten years' companionship;
  died, per ⁨الواقدي⁩, **at the end of ⁨معاوية ؓ⁩'s caliphate** `[SOURCED]` ⁨سیر ج۳ ص۱۰۳⁩ · ⁨ج۳ ص۱۱۲⁩. On the
  Line: from the Hijra to the end of ⁨معاوية ؓ⁩'s caliphate.
- `what he did` — he was **⁨علي ؓ⁩'s governor of Egypt** (36–37 AH), was removed on the strength of a
  forged letter, went back to ⁨علي ؓ⁩ anyway, commanded his **vanguard**, and after ⁨علي ؓ⁩'s death took
  safe-conduct for his five thousand men **and not for himself** `[SOURCED]` ⁨سیر ج۳ ص۱۰۳⁩ · ⁨ج۳ ص۱۰۹⁩ ·
  ج۳ ص۱۱۰–۱۱۱.

**Clan and kin.** **⁨الخزرج⁩**, of **⁨بني ساعدة⁩** — and that is the kinship the room needs above all
others, because his father is **⁨سعد بن عبادة ؓ⁩**, the chief of the Khazraj, the man of the
**⁨سقيفة بني ساعدة⁩** in session 1. ⁨الذهبي⁩'s own phrase, `⁨سَيِّدُ الخَزْرَجِ وَابْنُ سَيِّدِهِم⁩
⁨أَبِي ثَابِتٍ⁩`, makes the link `[SOURCED]` ⁨سیر ج۳ ص۱۰۲⁩. His wife was **⁨قُرَيبة⁩, the sister of
⁨الصديق ؓ⁩** `[SOURCED]` ⁨سیر ج۳ ص۱۰۷⁩.

**Before 23 AH.** ⁨أنس ؓ⁩: he stood to the Prophet ﷺ **as the chief of police stands to the commander**
— ⁨البخاري⁩ carries it `[SOURCED]` ⁨سیر ج۳ ص۱۰۳⁩. He carried the Prophet's ﷺ standard on some of his
expeditions `[SOURCED]` ⁨سیر ج۳ ص۱۰۳⁩–۱۰۴. He was huge, heavy, small-headed and beardless; when he rode
a donkey his feet drew lines in the ground `[SOURCED]` ⁨سیر ج۳ ص۱۰۳⁩. And he was famously open-handed:
on the expedition of ⁨سيف البحر⁩, when the men were dividing single dates, he bought camels on credit
against dates to be delivered in Medina, and slaughtered them for the army three days running until
his commander stopped him — ⁨عمر ؓ⁩ saying, all the while, `⁨يَا عَجَباً لِهَذَا الغُلاَمِ، يَدِيْنُ فِي⁩
⁨مَالِ غَيْرِهِ⁩` `[SOURCED]` ⁨سیر ج۳ ص۱۰۵⁩. His father wrote him four orchards for it, the least of them
yielding fifty wasqs `[SOURCED]` ⁨سیر ج۳ ص۱۰۶⁩; and it is said the Prophet ﷺ, told of it, said
`⁨أَمَا إِنَّهُ فِي بَيْتِ جُوْدٍ⁩` — *he is in a house of generosity* `[SOURCED]` ⁨سیر ج۳ ص۱۰۶⁩.

**In this window.** **⁨ابن يونس⁩**: he was present at the conquest of Egypt and laid out a house there;
**he governed it for ⁨علي ؓ⁩ in the year 36, and was removed from it in 37** `[SOURCED]` ⁨سیر ج۳ ص۱۰۳⁩.
**⁨يزيد بن أبي حبيب⁩**: he held Egypt firm, kept ⁨معاوية ؓ⁩ and ⁨عمرو ؓ⁩ off it by sheer manoeuvre, paid the
stipends generously, and **sent no grain to Syria** `[SOURCED]` ⁨سیر ج۳ ص۱۰۹⁩. ⁨الزهري⁩ and others counted
him among the **⁨دهاة العرب⁩** of the fitna `[SOURCED]` ⁨سیر ج۳ ص۱۰۸⁩ · ⁨ج۳ ص۲۲⁩.

**One ⁨واقعہ⁩ worth telling — the letter that was never written.** ⁨الذهبي⁩ gives the story from
⁨يزيد بن أبي حبيب⁩. Unable to move ⁨قيس ؓ⁩, ⁨معاوية ؓ⁩ **wrote a letter as though it were from ⁨قيس⁩ to
himself**, full of grief over what had been done to ⁨عثمان ؓ⁩ and of obedience. Then he called the
prayer in Damascus, preached, and had the letter read out — *this is ⁨قيس بن سعد⁩, the fang of the
Arabs; he has seen the matter clearly, and he has ordered grain to be sent to you; so pray for ⁨قيس⁩,
and raise your hands.* They raised their hands. Then ⁨معاوية ؓ⁩ said to ⁨عمرو ؓ⁩: *watch for the spies to
go out — in seven or eight days the news reaches ⁨علي⁩, and he removes ⁨قيس⁩; and anyone else who governs
Egypt will be easier for us.* And when the news reached ⁨علي ؓ⁩, the report says he **did not accept it
at first**, and then removed him `[SOURCED]` ⁨سیر ج۳ ص۱۰۹⁩. The command went to **⁨الأشتر⁩**, who died on
the road; then to **⁨محمد بن أبي بكر ؓ⁩**, who was killed there; and **⁨عمرو ؓ⁩ took the province**
`[SOURCED]` ⁨سیر ج۳ ص۱۰۹⁩.

**And the ⁨واقعہ⁩ that is the whole point of him.** Dismissed, he came to Medina, where **⁨مروان⁩** and
**⁨الأسود بن أبي البختري⁩** plotted to fall on him by night. Word reached him, and he said:

> وَاللهِ إِنَّ هَذَا لَقَبِيْحٌ (٢) أَنْ أُفَارِقَ عَلِيّاً وَإِنْ عَزَلَنِي، وَاللهِ لأَلْحَقَنَّ بِهِ.
> — ⁨سیر أعلام النبلاء ج۳ ص۱۱۰⁩ · https://shamela.ws/book/10906/2719
> *English:* "By God, this is an ugly thing — that I should part from ⁨علي⁩ merely because he removed
> me. By God, I shall join him."

He joined him, told him what he had been managing in Egypt, and ⁨علي ؓ⁩, understanding what he had been
holding off by stratagem, obeyed him in everything and **put him over the vanguard of his army**. And
⁨معاوية ؓ⁩ wrote to ⁨مروان⁩ and ⁨الأسود⁩ in reproach:

> أَمْدَدْتُمَا (٥) عَلِيّاً بِقَيْسٍ؟ وَاللهِ لَوْ أَمْدَدْتُمَاهُ بِمائَةِ أَلْفِ مُقَاتِلٍ، مَا كَانَ بِأَغْيَظَ عَلَيَّ مِنْ إِخْرَاجِكُمَا قَيْساً إِلَيْهِ.
> — ⁨سیر أعلام النبلاء ج۳ ص۱۱۰⁩ · https://shamela.ws/book/10906/2719
> *English:* "You have reinforced ⁨علي⁩ with ⁨قيس⁩? By God, had you reinforced him with a hundred
> thousand fighting men, it would not have galled me more than your driving ⁨قيس⁩ out to him."

`[SOURCED]` ⁨سیر ج۳ ص۱۱۰⁩.

**His own words.**

> لَوْلاَ أَنِّي سَمِعْتُ رَسُوْلَ اللهِ -صَلَّى اللَّهُ عَلَيْهِ وَسَلَّمَ - يَقُوْلُ: (المَكْرُ وَالخَدِيْعَةُ فِي النَّارِ) (٤)
> — ⁨سیر أعلام النبلاء ج۳ ص۱۰۷⁩ · https://shamela.ws/book/10906/2716
> لَكُنْتُ مِنْ أَمْكَرِ هَذِهِ الأُمَّةِ.
> — ⁨سیر أعلام النبلاء ج۳ ص۱۰۸⁩ · https://shamela.ws/book/10906/2717
> *English:* "Had I not heard the Messenger of God ﷺ say, *stratagem and deceit are in the Fire*, I
> would have been among the craftiest of this Umma."

(The round brackets are the printed edition's own, marking prophetic speech. The editor notes the
ḥadīth has several routes, each with some criticism, but that together they give the text a basis and
it is **⁨حسن⁩** `[SOURCED]` ⁨سیر ج۳ ص۱۰۷⁩, footnote (٤).) A second wording, from ⁨عمرو⁩ through ⁨ابن عيينة⁩:
`⁨لَوْلاَ الإِسْلاَمُ، لَمَكَرْتُ مَكْراً لاَ تُطِيْقُهُ العَرَبُ⁩` — *were it not for Islam, I would
have contrived something the Arabs could not withstand* `[SOURCED]` ⁨سیر ج۳ ص۱۰۸⁩. And a third, through
ابن سيرين: `لَوْلاَ أَنَّ المَكْرَ فُجُوْرٌ، لَمَكَرْتُ مَكْراً تَضْطَرِبُ مِنْهُ أَهْلُ الشَّامِ
⁨بَيْنَهُم⁩` `[SOURCED]` ⁨سیر ج۳ ص۱۰۸⁩.

**And the sentence to end on.** After ⁨علي ؓ⁩ was killed, ⁨قيس ؓ⁩ was still in the vanguard with **five
thousand men who had shaved their heads**. When ⁨الحسن ؓ⁩ entered into the pledge to ⁨معاوية ؓ⁩, ⁨قيس ؓ⁩
refused to enter, and said to his men: *if you wish, I will fight with you for ever, until whichever
of us is soonest dies; and if you wish, I will take safe conduct for you.* They said: take it for us.

> فَأَخَذَ لَهُم، وَلَمْ يَأْخُذْ لِنَفْسِهِ خَاصَّةً.
> — ⁨سیر أعلام النبلاء ج۳ ص۱۱۱⁩ · https://shamela.ws/book/10906/2720
> *English:* "So he took it for them — and took none for himself in particular."

`[SOURCED]` ⁨سیر ج۳ ص۱۱۰⁩–۱۱۱. **The same thing ⁨الأشعث بن قيس ؓ⁩ had done at the ⁨ردة⁩, about thirty years
earlier** (⁨سیر ج۲ ص۳۹⁩). And on the road home he slaughtered a camel for them every day as far as
**⁨صِرار⁩**, three miles from Medina `[SOURCED]` ⁨سیر ج۳ ص۱۱۱⁩.

**The date and manner of death.** `⁨قَالَ الوَاقِدِيُّ، وَغَيْرُهُ⁩: ⁨تُوُفِّيَ قَيْسٌ فِي آخِرِ⁩
⁨خِلاَفَةِ مُعَاوِيَةَ⁩` — **at the end of ⁨معاوية ؓ⁩'s caliphate** `[SOURCED]` ⁨سیر ج۳ ص۱۱۲⁩. **No year is
given. Do not supply one.** Elsewhere on the same ⁨ترجمه الواقدي⁩ says that when ⁨علي ؓ⁩ was killed, ⁨قيس ؓ⁩
went home `[SOURCED]` ⁨سیر ج۳ ص۱۰۲⁩.

**⚠ What not to say about him from the platform.** Two items. **(a)** The exchange in which ⁨معاوية ؓ⁩
calls him *a rabbi of the rabbis of the Jews* and ⁨قيس ؓ⁩ answers about idols — **⁨الذهبي⁩ himself writes
`⁨هَذَا مُنْقَطِعٌ⁩` immediately beneath it** `[SOURCED]` ⁨سیر ج۳ ص۱۱۱⁩. **(b)** The long exchange with
the Anṣārī delegation, in which each side reproaches the other over ⁨صفين⁩ `[SOURCED]` ⁨سیر⁩
⁨ج۳ ص۱۱۱⁩–۱۱۲ — real, recorded, and not for this room.

---

## What could NOT be established

- **The year of ⁨الأشتر⁩'s death.** ⁨سیر ج۴ ص۳۴⁩ gives the manner (poisoned on the road to Egypt after
  ⁨صفين⁩) and not the year. The usual date, 38 AH, is not on any page read for this note. `[SOURCED]`
  for the manner; **the year is left open**.
- **⁨الأشتر⁩'s birth, and any clan detail beyond ⁨النخع⁩.** Not on the page.
- **⁨صعصعة بن صوحان⁩'s death date, and his tribe by name.** ⁨سیر ج۳ ص۵۲۸⁩–۵۲۹ says only that he lived into
  ⁨معاوية ؓ⁩'s caliphate. His tribe (⁨عبد القيس⁩) is conventional and is **not** stated on this page.
- **Whether ⁨هاشم بن عتبة⁩ is a Companion.** ⁨الذهبي⁩ records only that *some* counted him one because he
  lived in the Prophet's ﷺ time, and himself closes with ⁨رحمہ اللہ⁩ (⁨سیر ج۳ ص۴۸۶⁩). We do not resolve
  it; we report it.
- **The number of ⁨حجر بن عدي⁩'s companions who were offered recantation.** ⁨الذهبي⁩ gives thirteen men,
  seven killed and six spared (⁨ص۴۶۵⁩) and then, under `⁨وَقِيْلَ⁩`, ten who refused and ten who accepted
  (⁨ص۴۶۶⁩). The two do not reconcile and ⁨الذهبي⁩ does not reconcile them.
- **Who killed ⁨محمد بن أبي بكر ؓ⁩.** Two accounts on one page: ⁨معاوية بن حديج⁩, and ⁨عمرو بن العاص ؓ⁩
  (⁨سیر ج۳ ص۴۸۲⁩). Not resolved.
- **Which of ⁨المغيرة ؓ⁩'s four accounts of losing his eye is correct** (⁨اليرموك⁩ / ⁨القادسية⁩ / ⁨الطائف⁩ /
  the eclipse). All four are in ⁨سیر⁩; none is preferred.
- **The verse `⁨اقْتُلُوْنِي وَمَالِكاً⁩`.** ⁨الذهبي⁩ attributes it to ⁨ابن الزبير ؓ⁩ against ⁨الأشتر⁩ with
  `⁨وَقِيْلَ⁩`; the editor notes ⁨الطبري ۴⁩/۵۲۰ puts it in the mouth of ⁨عبد الرحمن بن عتاب بن أسيد⁩ at
  ⁨الجمل⁩ (⁨سیر ج۴ ص۳۵⁩). Unresolved.
- **⁨الأشعث ؓ⁩'s age at death.** "It is said sixty-three" — ⁨سیر ج۲ ص۴۲⁩, and nothing firmer.
- **⁨قيس بن سعد ؓ⁩'s year of death.** "The end of ⁨معاوية ؓ⁩'s caliphate" and no more (⁨سیر ج۳ ص۱۱۲⁩).
- **Anything about ⁨صفين⁩ as a battle.** This note is a register of men. The sequence of the battle, the
  raising of the maṣāḥif, the document of arbitration and the emergence of the ⁨خوارج⁩ are **not**
  established here beyond the single sentence of ⁨الذهبي⁩ quoted in §4, and must come from the battle
  note, not from this one.
- **Nothing was taken from ⁨تاریخ الطبري⁩.** Where a page of ⁨سیر⁩ names ⁨الطبري⁩, ⁨الواقدي⁩, ⁨سيف بن عمر⁩ or
  ⁨ابن جرير⁩ as **its own** source, that is recorded as *the book's own attribution* and never as a
  citation of ours (`CLAUDE.md` §4).

---

## Not for the hall

Each of these is real, is on a page that was read, and is recorded here **so that it is never
re-researched** — and so that the speaker knows what is in the book when a written question from the
box quotes it back at him.

1. **⁨عمرو بن العاص ؓ⁩ and ⁨عمار ؓ⁩ at ⁨صفين⁩.** ⁨الحسن⁩'s report has a man press ⁨عمرو ؓ⁩ about two men the
   Prophet ﷺ loved when he died, one of them ⁨عمار ؓ⁩, and ⁨عمرو ؓ⁩ answer `⁨ذَاكَ قَتِيْلُكُم بِصِفِّيْنَ⁩`
   — *that is the man you killed at ⁨صفين⁩* — and, when pressed, `⁨قَدْ⁩ -⁨وَاللهِ⁩ - ⁨فَعَلْنَا⁩`. ⁨سیر⁩
   ⁨ج۳ ص۶۸⁩. **Why not:** it puts a Companion's killing in a Companion's mouth in front of a room that
   will not be able to hear it as ⁨تاریخ⁩.
2. **The black standard at ⁨صفين⁩.** A man who took the Prophet's ﷺ standard on condition
   `⁨لاَ تُقَاتِلُ بِهَا مُسْلِماً⁩` is said to have raised it at ⁨صفين⁩ — *and he is ⁨عمرو بن العاص⁩*.
   ⁨سیر ج۳ ص۶۸⁩. **Why not:** same reason, and the ending is a verdict the platform must not deliver.
3. **The ⁨شداد بن أوس ؓ⁩ report on ⁨عمرو ؓ⁩ and ⁨معاوية ؓ⁩.** ⁨سیر ج۳ ص۷۲⁩. **Why not:** ⁨ابن عساكر⁩, quoted on
   the page, says **⁨سعيد بن عبد الرحمن⁩ and his father are unknown** and that ⁨سعيد بن كثير بن عفير⁩ is
   weakened by others. A report about two Companions on an unknown chain does not go anywhere near a
   slide.
4. **The ⁨ابن عباس ؓ⁩ / ⁨عمرو ؓ⁩ / ⁨معاوية ؓ⁩ exchange over ⁨عثمان ؓ⁩.** ⁨سیر ج۳ ص۷۳⁩. Each man accuses the
   other in the plainest terms. **Why not:** it is the ⁨مشاجرات⁩ in a single paragraph.
5. **The charge brought against ⁨المغيرة ؓ⁩ at ⁨البصرة⁩.** ⁨سیر ج۳ ص۲۷⁩–۲۸. **Why not:** it is a moral
   accusation against a Companion; ⁨الذهبي⁩ himself notes that the long version comes from **⁨سيف بن عمر⁩
   and ⁨أبو حذيفة النجاري⁩, at length and with no chain**, and the editor adds that ⁨سيف⁩ is ⁨متروك⁩. If a
   question from the box raises it, the answer is exactly that sentence of ⁨الذهبي⁩'s and nothing else.
6. **`⁨كَانَ المُغِيْرَةُ يَنَالُ فِي خُطْبَتِهِ مِنْ عَلِيٍّ⁩`.** ⁨سیر ج۳ ص۳۱⁩. **Why not:** it is a
   flashpoint and it belongs, if anywhere, to the session that handles the Umayyad period with ⁨اصول⁩.
7. **⁨المغيرة ؓ⁩'s sentence about ⁨عثمان ؓ⁩ in the exchange with ⁨عمار ؓ⁩.** ⁨سیر ج۳ ص۲۹⁩. **Why not:**
   a Companion's judgement on a caliph, quoted at second hand.
8. **⁨حذيفة ؓ⁩'s reported `⁨أَحَدُهُمَا مُنَافِقٌ⁩`.** ⁨الأعمش⁩, from ⁨شقيق⁩: two men entered the mosque and
   ⁨حذيفة ؓ⁩ said one of the two was a hypocrite. **⁨الذهبي⁩'s own response is on the page**, and this is
   the most valuable single line in the note:

   > قُلْتُ: مَا أَدْرِي مَا وَجْهُ هَذَا القَوْلِ، سَمِعَهُ عَبْدُ اللهِ بنُ نُمَيْرٍ مِنْهُ، ثُمَّ يَقُوْلُ الأَعْمَشُ: حَدَّثْنَاهُمْ بِغَضَبِ أَصْحَابِ مُحَمَّدٍ -صَلَّى اللَّهُ عَلَيْهِ وَسَلَّمَ- فَاتَّخَذُوْهُ دِيْناً.
   > — ⁨سیر أعلام النبلاء ج۲ ص۳۹۴⁩ · https://shamela.ws/book/10906/2374
   > *English:* "I say: I do not know what the sense of this statement is. ⁨عبد الله بن نمير⁩ heard it
   > from him; and then ⁨الأعمش⁩ says: *we related to them what the Companions of Muḥammad ﷺ said in
   > anger, and they took it for a religion.*"

   The editor adds beneath it: `⁨فإن صح هذا عن حذيفة ولا إخاله يصح⁩` — *if this is sound from ⁨حذيفة⁩,
   and I do not think it is sound* `[SOURCED]` ⁨سیر ج۲ ص۳۹۴⁩ and footnote (١). **This is the model
   answer to any hostile question of the form "but didn't so-and-so say…": the authority himself says
   he does not know what it means, and the reporter himself says such words were said in anger and
   should never have been made into a creed.**
9. **⁨قيس بن سعد ؓ⁩ and ⁨معاوية ؓ⁩ — "a rabbi of the rabbis of the Jews".** ⁨سیر ج۳ ص۱۱۱⁩. **Why not:**
   ⁨الذهبي⁩ writes `⁨هَذَا مُنْقَطِعٌ⁩` directly beneath it. Record the rejection, not the report.
10. **The ⁨قيس ؓ⁩ / Anṣār delegation exchange before ⁨معاوية ؓ⁩.** ⁨سیر ج۳ ص۱۱۱⁩–۱۱۲, with its reproaches
    over ⁨صفين⁩ on both sides. **Why not:** it is the ⁨مشاجرات⁩ again, and in the sharpest possible
    register.
11. **The ⁨قيصر⁩ / ⁨سراويل⁩ story about ⁨قيس ؓ⁩.** ⁨سیر ج۳ ص۱۱۲⁩. **Why not:** the editor states flatly
    `⁨وهو باطل كما في⁩ " ⁨الاستيعاب⁩ "` — *it is false, as in ⁨الاستيعاب⁩*. It is entertaining and it is
    rejected; it is listed here so that nobody re-finds it and uses it.
12. **⁨عمرو ؓ⁩'s characterisation of the ⁨أمصار⁩** (⁨الشام⁩, ⁨مصر⁩, ⁨الحجاز⁩, ⁨العراق⁩). ⁨سیر ج۳ ص۵۷⁩. **Why not:**
    the editor marks both ⁨أبو أمية بن يعلى⁩ and ⁨علي بن زيد⁩ weak and says `⁨فالخبر لا يصح⁩` — the report is
    not sound. It is quotable, memorable, and unusable.
13. **The gold ring of ⁨حذيفة ؓ⁩.** ⁨سیر ج۲ ص۳۶۷⁩. **Why not:** the sole narrator ⁨أم موسى⁩ is unknown, and
    the prohibition of gold for men is established — the editor says so on the page.
14. **⁨محمد بن أبي بكر ؓ⁩'s body.** ⁨سیر ج۳ ص۴۸۲⁩ · ⁨ج۳ ص۱۱۰⁩. The manner of the killing is stated; **the
    burning is not narrated from the platform.**

---

## EVENT CARDS

---

### E-PG1 · The shrewdest head in Quraysh

**Tier:** CORE · **When:** 8–43 AH `[SOURCED]` · **Map:** Mecca, then Medina, then Egypt — light the Nile delta.

**What happened:** ⁨عمرو بن العاص ؓ⁩ was of ⁨بنو سهم⁩, one of the smaller houses of Quraysh. He came to Islam early in the eighth year, travelling with ⁨خالد بن الوليد ؓ⁩, and the Prophet ﷺ put him in command almost at once. He was older than ⁨عمر ؓ⁩ and used to say he remembered the night ⁨عمر⁩ was born. He conquered Egypt, governed it for ⁨عمر ؓ⁩ and for the first part of ⁨عثمان ؓ⁩'s caliphate, lost it, and governed it again at the end of his life. ⁨الذهبي⁩ heads his entry with one word above all the others: the shrewd one of Quraysh.

**The statement:**
> دَاهِيَةُ قُرَيْشٍ، وَرَجُلُ العَالَمِ، وَمَنْ يُضْرَبُ بِهِ المَثَلُ فِي الفِطْنَةِ، وَالدَّهَاءِ، وَالحَزْمِ.
> — ⁨سیر أعلام النبلاء ج۳ ص۵۵⁩ · https://shamela.ws/book/10906/2664
> *English:* "The shrewd one of Quraysh, and the man of the age, and the one who is made a byword for quickness, for shrewdness and for resolve."

**⁨عبرت⁩:** A man may be introduced by the thing he is best at, and that thing may be the very thing that later decides him.

**Hands-up?** no

---

### E-PG2 · Two pulpits outside Alexandria

**Tier:** CORE · **When:** at the siege of Alexandria; the page gives the conquest as 21 AH `[SOURCED]`, and dates the dialogue to it only by inference `[CONVENTIONAL-ESTIMATE]` · **Map:** Alexandria on the Egyptian coast.

**What happened:** The Muslim army was camped before Alexandria when a Byzantine notable called across the lines for one man to come out and speak with him. ⁨عمرو ؓ⁩ said no one would go but himself. Two pulpits were set up, each man with his interpreter. Asked who the Arabs were, ⁨عمرو ؓ⁩ answered without decorating anything: we were the narrowest of people in land and the worst in living, we ate carrion and raided one another, until a man rose among us who was neither our noblest nor our richest and said, I am the Messenger of God to you. The Byzantine laughed and said that messengers had come to his own people with the same thing, and that they had kept to it until kings rose among them who did as they pleased and left the affair of the prophets. Then he told ⁨عمرو ؓ⁩ what would happen if the Arabs did the same.

**The statement:**
> فَإِنْ أَنْتُم أَخَذْتُمْ بِأَمْرِ نَبِيِّكُمْ، لَمْ يُقَاتِلْكُمْ أَحَدٌ إِلاَّ غَلَبْتُمُوْهُ، وَإِذَا فَعَلْتُم مِثْلَ الَّذِي فَعَلْنَا، فَتَرَكْتُمْ أَمَرَ نَبِيِّكُم، لَمْ تَكُوْنُوا أَكْثَرَ عَدَداً مِنَّا، وَلاَ أَشَدَّ مِنَّا قُوَّةً
> — ⁨سیر أعلام النبلاء ج۳ ص۷۱⁩ · https://shamela.ws/book/10906/2680
> *English:* "If you hold to the command of your Prophet, no one will fight you but you will beat him; and if you do as we did and abandon the command of your Prophet, you will be no more numerous than we were, nor any stronger."

**⁨عبرت⁩:** The warning that was worth most to the Muslims in the hour of their victory was given to them by the man they had just defeated.

**Hands-up?** Before telling it, ask: who here has heard a warning from someone who had every reason not to give it?

---

### E-PG3 · Egypt taken, Egypt lost, Egypt given back

**Tier:** GOOD · **When:** 21–43 AH `[SOURCED]` · **Map:** Egypt, held then handed on three times — Alexandria, Tripoli of the West.

**What happened:** ⁨عمر ؓ⁩ gave ⁨عمرو ؓ⁩ Palestine and Jordan, then wrote to him to move on Egypt, and sent ⁨الزبير ؓ⁩ after him with reinforcements. Alexandria fell in the twenty-first year and revolted again in the twenty-fifth. Tripoli of the West was taken in the twenty-fourth year, and some say the twenty-third. Then ⁨عثمان ؓ⁩ removed him and appointed ⁨عبد الله بن أبي سرح⁩ in his place. Years later ⁨معاوية ؓ⁩ gave the province back to him, and he entered it as its governor in the thirty-ninth year, held it about three years, and died there.

**The statement:**
> وَقَالَ ابْنُ لَهِيْعَةَ: فَتَحَ عَمْرُو بنُ العَاصِ الإِسْكَنْدَرِيَّةَ سَنَةَ إِحْدَى وَعِشْرِيْنَ، ثُمَّ انَتَقَضُوا فِي سَنَةِ خَمْسٍ وَعِشْرِيْنَ.
> — ⁨سیر أعلام النبلاء ج۳ ص۷۰⁩ · https://shamela.ws/book/10906/2679
> *English:* "⁨ابن لهيعة⁩ said: ⁨عمرو بن العاص⁩ conquered Alexandria in the year twenty-one, and then they broke the treaty in the year twenty-five."

**⁨عبرت⁩:** A province can be won by one man and still not belong to him.

**Hands-up?** no

---

### E-PG4 · Weigh my money out for me

**Tier:** GOOD · **When:** 43 AH `[SOURCED]` · **Map:** No map.

**What happened:** At the end, in Egypt, ⁨عمرو ؓ⁩ asked for his money to be measured out. They measured it and found fifty-two mudds of it. He looked at it and asked who would take it with what came attached to it, and said he wished it had been camel dung. Then he ordered his guard to surround the palace, and when his sons asked what that was for, he said: do you see this keeping anything away from me at all. And he called the guard in and told them to keep death off him, and when they said they had not thought to hear him say such a thing, he said he knew it perfectly well.

**The statement:**
> مَنْ يَأْخُذُهُ بِمَا فِيْهِ؟ يَا لَيْتَهُ كَانَ بَعْراً.
> — ⁨سیر أعلام النبلاء ج۳ ص۷۴⁩ · https://shamela.ws/book/10906/2683
> *English:* "Who will take it with what is in it? Would that it had been camel dung."

**⁨عبرت⁩:** A man is allowed to say at the end of his life what he could not say in the middle of it, and the saying is still worth hearing.

**Hands-up?** no

---

### E-PG5 · ⁨الذهبي⁩ weighs ⁨عمرو ؓ⁩, and stops

**Tier:** CORE · **When:** written in the eighth century AH `[SOURCED]` · **Map:** No map.

**What happened:** The most useful sentence in this whole evening is not about an event at all. It is the way a Sunni authority of the first rank writes about a Companion he has reservations about. ⁨الذهبي⁩ sets down the praise in full, asks God's forgiveness for him in the same breath, names the reservation plainly, and then closes with a fact that cuts the other way: that ⁨عمرو ؓ⁩ has a precedence in Islam which ⁨معاوية ؓ⁩ does not have. He does not adjudicate, and he does not soften. That is the register of the whole session.

**The statement:**
> وَكَانَ مِنْ رِجَالِ قُرَيْشٍ رَأْياً، وَدَهَاءً، وَحَزْماً، وَكَفَاءةً، وَبَصَراً بِالحُرُوْبِ، وَمِنْ أَشْرَافِ مُلُوْكِ العَرَبِ، وَمِنْ أَعْيَانِ المُهَاجِرِيْنَ، وَاللهُ يَغْفِرُ لَهُ وَيَعفُو عَنْهُ، وَلَوْلاَ حُبُّهُ لِلدُّنْيَا، وَدُخُوْلُهُ فِي أُمُوْرٍ، لَصَلُحَ لِلْخِلاَفَةِ، فَإِنَّ لَهُ سَابِقَةً لَيْسَتْ لِمُعَاوِيَةَ.
> — ⁨سیر أعلام النبلاء ج۳ ص۵۹⁩ · https://shamela.ws/book/10906/2668
> *English:* "He was among the men of Quraysh in judgement, shrewdness, resolve, competence and insight into war; among the noblest of the kings of the Arabs and among the chief of the Muhājirūn — may God forgive him and pardon him. Had it not been for his love of this world and his entering into certain affairs, he would have been fit for the caliphate, for he has a precedence that ⁨معاوية⁩ does not have."

**⁨عبرت⁩:** Praise and reservation can stand in one sentence without either of them becoming a verdict.

**Hands-up?** no

---

### E-PG6 · The man who taught Basra its Qurʾān

**Tier:** CORE · **When:** 7–44 AH `[SOURCED]` · **Map:** Yemen, then Medina, then Basra and Kufa — light the two garrison cities.

**What happened:** ⁨أبو موسى الأشعري ؓ⁩, whose name was ⁨عبد الله بن قيس⁩, was neither of Quraysh nor of the Anṣār. He came from Yemen with a party of his own people and reached Medina in the days of ⁨خيبر⁩, and the Prophet ﷺ told the Ashʿarīs they had emigrated twice. He was sent with ⁨معاذ ؓ⁩ to Yemen. ⁨عمر ؓ⁩ made him governor of Basra, where he taught the people their Qurʾān and their fiqh, and from which he took ⁨الأهواز⁩, ⁨تستر⁩ and ⁨أصبهان⁩. ⁨مسروق⁩ counts him one of the six men of judgement among the Companions. No Companion had a finer voice.

**The statement:**
> الإِمَامُ الكَبِيْرُ،
> — ⁨سیر أعلام النبلاء ج۲ ص۳۸۰⁩ · https://shamela.ws/book/10906/2360
> صَاحِبُ رَسُوْلِ اللهِ -صَلَّى اللَّهُ عَلَيْهِ وَسَلَّمَ- أَبُو مُوْسَى الأَشْعَرِيُّ، التَّمِيْمِيُّ، الفَقِيْهُ، المُقْرِئُ.
> — ⁨سیر أعلام النبلاء ج۲ ص۳۸۱⁩ · https://shamela.ws/book/10906/2361
> *English:* "The great Imām, the Companion of the Messenger of God ﷺ, ⁨أبو موسى الأشعري⁩, ⁨التميمي⁩, the jurist, the Qurʾān-reciter."
>
> *Flag:* the printed edition reads **⁨التَّمِيْمِيُّ⁩** here, where the nisba one would expect is
> ⁨الأشعري⁩'s own. The text is kept as the page has it and is not corrected.

**⁨عبرت⁩:** The teaching of a city outlasts the governing of it.

**Hands-up?** no

---

### E-PG7 · Six hundred dirhams out of Basra

**Tier:** CORE · **When:** 23–35 AH `[SOURCED]` · **Map:** Basra.

**What happened:** ⁨أبو موسى ؓ⁩ governed Basra for ⁨عمر ؓ⁩, and ⁨عمر ؓ⁩, who wrote into his will that no governor of his should be kept in post more than a year, made one exception in writing: keep the Ashʿarī four. Word once reached him that people were staying away from the Friday prayer because they had no clothes to come in, so he went out to them in a rough cloak. When ⁨عثمان ؓ⁩ removed him from Basra, his own daughter-in-law told his son what he had come out with. He had governed the richest province in Iraq.

**The statement:**
> خَرَجَ أَبُو مُوْسَى حِيْنَ نُزِعَ عَنِ البَصْرَةِ، مَا مَعَهُ إِلاَّ سِتُّ مائَةِ دِرْهَمٍ عَطَاءً لِعِيَالِهِ.
> — ⁨سیر أعلام النبلاء ج۲ ص۳۹۸⁩ · https://shamela.ws/book/10906/2378
> *English:* "⁨أبو موسى⁩ went out when he was removed from Basra with nothing on him but six hundred dirhams — the stipend for his family."

**⁨عبرت⁩:** The account of a man's governorship is settled on the day he leaves it, not on the day he takes it.

**Hands-up?** Before telling it, ask: what would you expect a governor to leave office with?

---

### E-PG8 · The letter he did not answer

**Tier:** CORE · **When:** 37–40 AH `[SOURCED]` · **Map:** No map.

**What happened:** ⁨معاوية ؓ⁩ wrote to ⁨أبو موسى ؓ⁩ in his own hand. ⁨عمرو بن العاص⁩, he said, has already given me his pledge on what I want; and I swear that if you give me the same, I shall put one of your two sons over Kufa and the other over Basra, and no door will ever be shut in your face and no need of yours go unmet. He asked for the reply in ⁨أبو موسى ؓ⁩'s own hand, and he got it — four lines, and no fifth. The ending of the report is worth as much as the letter: when ⁨معاوية ؓ⁩ did come to power, ⁨أبو بردة⁩ says he went in to him, and no door was shut against him and no need of his went unmet.

**The statement:**
> أَمَّا بَعْدُ، فَإِنَّكَ كَتَبْتَ إِلَيَّ فِي جَسِيْمِ أَمْرِ الأُمَّةِ، فَمَاذَا أَقُوْلُ لِرَبِّي إِذَا قَدِمْتُ عَلَيْهِ، لَيْسَ لِي فِيْمَا عَرَضْتَ مِنْ حَاجَةٍ، وَالسَّلاَمُ عَلَيْكَ.
> — ⁨سیر أعلام النبلاء ج۲ ص۳۹۶⁩ · https://shamela.ws/book/10906/2376
> *English:* "To proceed: you have written to me about a grave matter of this Umma. What shall I say to my Lord when I come before Him? I have no need of what you have offered. And peace be upon you."

**⁨عبرت⁩:** A refusal costs nothing if the man refusing has already decided what he will say on a later day.

**Hands-up?** no

---

### E-PG9 · ⁨التحكيم⁩, in one sentence, from ⁨الذهبي⁩

**Tier:** CORE · **When:** 37 AH `[SOURCED]` · **Map:** ⁨دومة الجندل⁩ between Iraq and Syria.

**What happened:** This is the point in the evening where the speaker hands the whole matter over to the authority and stands back. ⁨الذهبي⁩ states the sequence and nothing beyond it: ⁨أبو موسى ؓ⁩ did not fight with ⁨علي ؓ⁩; ⁨علي ؓ⁩ then made him arbitrator over his own case; he deposed ⁨علي ؓ⁩ and deposed ⁨معاوية ؓ⁩ and proposed ⁨ابن عمر ؓ⁩; and out of that no settled state of affairs came. Then, on a later page, ⁨الذهبي⁩ writes his own verdict on the man himself, and it is unreserved.

**The statement:**
> وَلاَ رَيْبَ أَنَّ غُلاَةَ الشِّيْعَةِ يُبْغِضُوْنَ أَبَا مُوْسَى -رَضِيَ اللهُ عَنْهُ- لِكَوْنِهِ مَا قَاتَلَ مَعَ عَلِيٍّ، ثُمَّ لَمَّا حَكَّمَهُ عَلِيٌّ عَلَى نَفْسِهِ عَزَلَهُ، وَعَزَلَ مُعَاوِيَةَ، وَأَشَارَ بِابْنِ عُمَرَ؛ فَمَا انْتَظَمَ مِنْ ذَلِكَ حَالٌ.
> — ⁨سیر أعلام النبلاء ج۲ ص۳۹۴⁩ · https://shamela.ws/book/10906/2374
> *English:* "There is no doubt that the extreme Shīʿa hold ⁨أبو موسى ؓ⁩ in hatred, because he did not fight alongside ⁨علي⁩, and then, when ⁨علي⁩ made him arbitrator over his own case, he deposed him and deposed ⁨معاوية⁩ and proposed ⁨ابن عمر⁩ — and no settled state of affairs came out of that."

**⁨عبرت⁩:** When a matter has no settled outcome, saying so is the whole of the honest account.

**Hands-up?** no

### E-PG10 · Horses at the end of the course

**Tier:** GOOD · **When:** 44 AH `[SOURCED]` · **Map:** No map.

**What happened:** Near the end of his life ⁨أبو موسى ؓ⁩ worked himself very hard in worship. He was told he should hold back a little and go easier on himself. He answered with an image from the racecourse, which everyone in that world understood at once.

**The statement:**
> إِنَّ الخَيْلَ إِذَا أُرْسِلَتْ فَقَارَبَتْ رَأْسَ مَجْرَاهَا، أَخْرَجَتْ جَمِيْعَ مَا عِنْدَهَا؛ وَالَّذِي بَقِيَ مِنْ أَجَلِي أَقَلُّ مِنْ ذَلِكَ.
> — ⁨سیر أعلام النبلاء ج۲ ص۳۹۳⁩ · https://shamela.ws/book/10906/2373
> *English:* "When horses are let loose and come near the end of the course, they bring out everything they have. What is left of my appointed term is less than that."

**⁨عبرت⁩:** The last stretch is run harder than the first, not easier.

**Hands-up?** no

### E-PG11 · The man who was shrewdness itself

**Tier:** CORE · **When:** 6–50 AH `[SOURCED]` · **Map:** Ṭāʾif, then Medina, then Basra and Kufa.

**What happened:** ⁨المغيرة بن شعبة ؓ⁩ was of ⁨ثقيف⁩ of ⁨الطائف⁩, of the half of the tribe called ⁨الأحلاف⁩, and the nephew of ⁨عروة بن مسعود ؓ⁩. He was present at ⁨بيعة الرضوان⁩. He was tall and imposing, and he lost an eye — the sources give four different accounts of where. ⁨عمر ؓ⁩ set him over Bahrain, then Basra, then Kufa. He withdrew from both sides in the fitna, led the pilgrimage in the year ⁨علي ؓ⁩ was killed, and then governed Kufa for ⁨معاوية ؓ⁩ until he died in office. In Kufa they called him ⁨المغيرة⁩ of judgement.

**The statement:**
> مِنْ كِبَارِ الصَّحَابَةِ أُوْلِي الشَّجَاعَةِ وَالمَكِيْدَةِ. شَهِدَ بَيْعَةَ الرُّضْوَانِ.
> — ⁨سیر أعلام النبلاء ج۳ ص۲۱⁩ · https://shamela.ws/book/10906/2630
> *English:* "One of the senior Companions, of those possessed of courage and of stratagem. He was present at the Pledge of Riḍwān."

**⁨عبرت⁩:** A quality the sources name plainly in a man is a quality he was known for in his lifetime, not a charge invented afterwards.

**Hands-up?** no

### E-PG12 · There is no good in treachery

**Tier:** CORE · **When:** before the conquest of Mecca; the page gives no year `[CONVENTIONAL-ESTIMATE]` · **Map:** Ṭāʾif to Egypt and back to Medina.

**What happened:** Before Islam ⁨المغيرة ؓ⁩ went to Egypt with a party of ⁨بنو مالك⁩, the other half of ⁨ثقيف⁩. They were honoured there and he was slighted; nobody offered him a share of what they were given. On the road home they were drinking, and he killed thirteen of them and took what they had. He came straight to Medina, entered the mosque in his travelling clothes, and became Muslim. Then ⁨أبو بكر ؓ⁩ asked him what had become of the Mālikīs, and he answered that he had killed them and brought their spoils for the Prophet ﷺ to take the fifth of. The answer he got is the reason this card exists.

**The statement:**
> أَمَّا إِسْلاَمُكَ فَنَقْبَلُهُ، وَلاَ آخُذُ مِنْ أَمْوَالِهِم شَيْئاً، لأَنَّ هَذَا غَدْرٌ، وَلاَ خَيْرَ فِي الغَدْرِ
> — ⁨سیر أعلام النبلاء ج۳ ص۲۵⁩ · https://shamela.ws/book/10906/2634
> *English:* "As for your Islam, we accept it; but I will take nothing of their property, because this was treachery, and there is no good in treachery."

**⁨عبرت⁩:** A man may be accepted whole and his method refused entire, in the same breath.

**Hands-up?** Before telling it, ask: if a man came to you with his conversion in one hand and stolen goods in the other, which would you take?

---

### E-PG13 · Ten men before the Persian commander

**Tier:** CORE · **When:** the day of ⁨القادسية⁩ `[SOURCED]`; the year 15 AH `[CONVENTIONAL-ESTIMATE]` · **Map:** ⁨القادسية⁩, at the edge of the Sawād of Iraq.

**What happened:** On the day of ⁨القادسية المغيرة ؓ⁩ went out with ten men to the Persian commander. The Persian told him that his people did not wish to kill the Arabs, because Arab corpses would defile their land. ⁨المغيرة ؓ⁩ answered that they had been worshipping stones until God sent them a Messenger and they followed him; that they had not come looking for food but had been ordered to fight their enemy; and that since there was food and water in this land in plenty, they would not leave until it belonged to both peoples. The Persian said: he has told the truth. And ⁨المغيرة ؓ⁩ answered him with a sentence that came true the next day.

**The statement:**
> فَقَالَ العِلْجُ: صَدَقَ. قَالَ: وَأَنْتَ تُفْقَأُ عَيْنُكَ غَداً. فَفُقِئَتْ عَيْنُهُ بِسَهْمٍ.
> — ⁨سیر أعلام النبلاء ج۳ ص۳۲⁩ · https://shamela.ws/book/10906/2641
> *English:* "The foreigner said: he has told the truth. He said: and tomorrow your eye will be put out. And his eye was put out by an arrow."

**⁨عبرت⁩:** An embassy is answered by what the man says he came for, not by what the other side fears he came for.

**Hands-up?** no

---

### E-PG14 · The hundred thousand and the district chief

**Tier:** GOOD · **When:** in ⁨عمر ؓ⁩'s caliphate `[SOURCED]` · **Map:** Bahrain.

**What happened:** ⁨عمر ؓ⁩ set ⁨المغيرة ؓ⁩ over Bahrain. The people disliked him and ⁨عمر ؓ⁩ removed him, and then they were afraid he would send him back. Their chief told them he could prevent it: collect a hundred thousand, and I will carry it to ⁨عمر⁩ and say ⁨المغيرة⁩ embezzled it and handed it to me. They collected it and he went and said it. ⁨عمر ؓ⁩ called ⁨المغيرة ؓ⁩ and put it to him, and ⁨المغيرة ؓ⁩ said the man had lied, that it had been two hundred thousand. ⁨عمر ؓ⁩ asked him what had made him do it, and he said: family and need. Then ⁨عمر ؓ⁩ turned to the foreigner, who swore he had never been given anything, small or large. And ⁨عمر ؓ⁩ turned back and asked ⁨المغيرة ؓ⁩ what he had meant by all of this.

**The statement:**
> قَالَ: الخَبِيْثُ كَذَبَ عَلَيَّ، فَأَحْبَبْتُ أَنْ أُخْزِيَهُ.
> — ⁨سیر أعلام النبلاء ج۳ ص۲۷⁩ · https://shamela.ws/book/10906/2636
> *English:* "He said: the wretch lied about me, and I wanted to shame him."

**⁨عبرت⁩:** A lie can be broken by agreeing with it further than the liar is prepared to go.

**Hands-up?** no

---

### E-PG15 · Removed from Basra, given Kufa

**Tier:** GOOD · **When:** in ⁨عمر ؓ⁩'s caliphate `[SOURCED]` · **Map:** Basra and Kufa side by side.

**What happened:** ⁨المغيرة ؓ⁩ was removed from Basra and then appointed over Kufa. The people of Iraq made a proverb out of it, and it is the kind of line that tells a room more about the running of an empire than a page of description would. ⁨ابن سيرين⁩ records that one man would say it to another as a curse.

**The statement:**
> كَانَ الرَّجُلُ يَقُوْلُ لِلآخَرِ: غَضِبَ اللهُ عَلَيْك كَمَا غَضِبَ أَمِيْرُ المُؤْمِنِيْنَ عَلَى المُغِيْرَةِ، عَزَلَهُ عَنِ البَصْرَةِ، فَوَلاَّهُ الكُوْفَةَ.
> — ⁨سیر أعلام النبلاء ج۳ ص۲۸⁩ · https://shamela.ws/book/10906/2637
> *English:* "A man would say to another: may God be angry with you as the Commander of the Faithful was angry with ⁨المغيرة⁩ — he removed him from Basra and appointed him over Kufa."

**⁨عبرت⁩:** The people who live under a government make their own summary of it, and it is usually shorter than ours.

**Hands-up?** no

---

### E-PG16 · Five shrewd men, and where each of them stood

**Tier:** CORE · **When:** 36–37 AH `[SOURCED]` · **Map:** Kufa, Syria, Ṭāʾif — put one marker on each man.

**What happened:** ⁨الزهري⁩ reduced the whole politics of the fitna to five names and their tribes, and then, in one further clause, said where each of them ended up. Two of the five were with ⁨علي ؓ⁩. One was with ⁨معاوية ؓ⁩. One withdrew altogether. It is the cleanest single sentence in the sources for showing the room that this was not two blocks of people but a small number of very capable men making separate choices.

**The statement:**
> كَانَ دُهَاةُ النَّاسِ فِي الفِتْنَةِ خَمْسَةً، فَمِنْ قُرَيْشٍ: عَمْرٌو، وَمُعَاوِيَةُ، وَمِنَ الأَنْصَارِ: قَيْسُ بنُ سَعْدٍ، وَمِنْ ثَقِيْفٍ:
> — ⁨سیر أعلام النبلاء ج۳ ص۲۲⁩ · https://shamela.ws/book/10906/2631
> المُغِيْرَةُ، وَمِنَ المُهَاجِرِيْنَ: عَبْدُ اللهِ بنُ بُدَيْلِ بنِ وَرْقَاءَ الخُزَاعِيُّ. فَكَانَ مَعَ عَلِيٍّ: قَيْسٌ، وَابْنُ بُدَيْلٍ، وَاعْتَزَلَ المُغِيْرَةُ بنُ شُعْبَةَ (١) .
> — ⁨سیر أعلام النبلاء ج۳ ص۲۳⁩ · https://shamela.ws/book/10906/2632
> *English:* "The shrewdest of people in the fitna were five: from Quraysh, ⁨عمرو⁩ and ⁨معاوية⁩; from the Anṣār, ⁨قيس بن سعد⁩; from ⁨ثقيف⁩, ⁨المغيرة⁩; and from the Muhājirūn, ⁨عبد الله بن بديل بن ورقاء الخزاعي⁩. ⁨قيس⁩ and ⁨ابن بديل⁩ were with ⁨علي⁩, and ⁨المغيرة بن شعبة⁩ withdrew."

**⁨عبرت⁩:** Men of comparable ability, given the same facts, went five different ways — which is why the tradition forbids us to read a man's choice as a verdict on him.

**Hands-up?** Before telling it, ask the room to guess how many men, in the end, the whole thing turned on.

---

### E-PG17 · The pilgrimage nobody was sent to lead

**Tier:** GOOD · **When:** 40 AH `[SOURCED]` · **Map:** Mecca, with Kufa and Syria both dark.

**What happened:** In the year ⁨علي ؓ⁩ was killed, the two sides were so taken up with each other that neither of them sent anyone to the pilgrimage season. ⁨المغيرة ؓ⁩, who had withdrawn and was living at ⁨الطائف⁩, simply came down, led the people in prayer, and invoked God for ⁨معاوية ؓ⁩. ⁨الليث⁩ records the year plainly. It is a small fact and it shows the room, better than any description of a battle, what a civil war does to the ordinary running of the religion.

**The statement:**
> قَالَ اللَّيْثُ: وَحَجَّ بِالنَّاسِ المُغِيْرَةُ سَنَةَ أَرْبَعِيْنَ.
> — ⁨سیر أعلام النبلاء ج۳ ص۲۹⁩ · https://shamela.ws/book/10906/2638
> *English:* "⁨الليث⁩ said: and ⁨المغيرة⁩ led the people in the pilgrimage in the year forty."

**⁨عبرت⁩:** When those in charge are busy with one another, the thing they were in charge of is left for whoever turns up.

**Hands-up?** no

---

### E-PG18 · The governor of Kufa dies in office

**Tier:** GOOD · **When:** ⁨شعبان⁩ 50 AH `[SOURCED]` · **Map:** Kufa.

**What happened:** ⁨المغيرة ؓ⁩ died in ⁨شعبان⁩ of the fiftieth year, seventy years old, still governor of Kufa. ⁨جرير بن عبد الله ؓ⁩ stood up and told the people to fear God, to listen and obey until a governor came to them, and to ask forgiveness for ⁨المغيرة⁩. And ⁨زياد⁩ stood over his grave and recited two lines that had been made four centuries earlier by ⁨مهلهل⁩ over his own brother.

**The statement:**
> أُوْصِيْكُم بِتَقْوَى اللهِ، وَأَنْ تَسْمَعُوا وَتُطِيْعُوا حَتَّى يَأْتِيَكُم أَمِيْرٌ، اسْتَغْفِرُوا لِلْمُغِيْرَةِ، غَفَرَ اللهُ لَهُ، فَإِنَّهُ كَانَ يُحِبُّ العَافِيَةَ.
> — ⁨سیر أعلام النبلاء ج۳ ص۳۱⁩ · https://shamela.ws/book/10906/2640
> *English:* "I charge you with the fear of God, and that you listen and obey until a governor comes to you. Ask forgiveness for ⁨المغيرة⁩ — may God forgive him — for he loved to be let alone."

**⁨عبرت⁩:** The words said over a man on the day he dies are chosen by someone who knew him, and are worth more than our summary of him.

**Hands-up?** no

---

### E-PG19 · ⁨عمر ؓ⁩ looks at a young man and says one sentence

**Tier:** CORE · **When:** in ⁨عمر ؓ⁩'s caliphate `[SOURCED]` · **Map:** No map.

**What happened:** ⁨مالك بن الحارث النخعي⁩, known as ⁨الأشتر⁩, was not a Companion. He was of ⁨النخع⁩, a Yemeni clan settled in Kufa, and he learned from ⁨عمر ؓ⁩ and from ⁨خالد بن الوليد ؓ⁩. He lost an eye at ⁨اليرموك⁩. He was sharp, he was obeyed, he was rough-tempered, and he was a powerful speaker. ⁨عبد الله بن سلمة المرادي⁩ reports that ⁨عمر ؓ⁩ once looked him up and down, and then down and up again, and said something about him that nobody understood at the time.

**The statement:**
> إِنَّ لِلْمُسْلِمِيْنَ مِنْ هَذَا يَوْماً عَصِيْباً.
> — ⁨سیر أعلام النبلاء ج۴ ص۳۴⁩ · https://shamela.ws/book/10906/3187
> *English:* "The Muslims will have a hard day from this man."

**⁨عبرت⁩:** Capacity and danger are often the same quality seen at two different moments.

**Hands-up?** no

---

### E-PG20 · Stopped by his own side

**Tier:** CORE · **When:** ⁨صفين⁩, 37 AH `[SOURCED]` · **Map:** ⁨صفين⁩ on the upper Euphrates.

**What happened:** At ⁨صفين الأشتر⁩ was the most effective commander on ⁨علي ؓ⁩'s side, and by the end of the fighting he had come within reach of breaking ⁨معاوية ؓ⁩'s line. Then the Syrians raised the leaves of the Qurʾān on their spear-points and called for the Book of God to judge between them. The men of ⁨علي ؓ⁩'s own army turned round and bore down on ⁨الأشتر⁩ to make him stop. He could not disobey ⁨علي ؓ⁩, so he held back. One sentence in ⁨الذهبي⁩ carries the whole of it, and with it the reason the arbitration happened at all.

**The statement:**
> شَهِدَ صِفِّيْنَ (٢) مَعَ عَلِيٍّ، وَتَمَّيْزَ يَوْمَئِذٍ، وَكَادَ أَنْ يَهْزِمَ مُعَاوِيَةَ، فَحَمَلَ عَلَيْهِ أَصْحَابُ عَلِيٍّ لَمَّا رَأَوْا مُصْحَفَ جُنْدِ الشَّامِ عَلَى الأَسِنَّةِ يَدْعُوْنَ إِلَى كِتَابِ اللهِ. وَمَا أَمْكَنَهُ مُخَالَفَةُ عَلِيٍّ، فَكَفَّ.
> — ⁨سیر أعلام النبلاء ج۴ ص۳۴⁩ · https://shamela.ws/book/10906/3187
> *English:* "He was present at ⁨صفين⁩ with ⁨علي⁩ and distinguished himself that day, and came near to routing ⁨معاوية⁩ — and then ⁨علي⁩'s own companions bore down on him, when they saw the Muṣḥaf of the Syrian army raised on the spear-points calling to the Book of God. It was not open to him to disobey ⁨علي⁩, so he held back."

**⁨عبرت⁩:** An army can be halted by its own men more completely than by the enemy in front of it.

**Hands-up?** Before telling it, ask: what would you do if the other side lifted the Qurʾān above their heads?

---

### E-PG21 · A road to Egypt and a jar of honey

**Tier:** GOOD · **When:** after ⁨صفين⁩ `[SOURCED]` · **Map:** the road from Iraq to Egypt.

**What happened:** When ⁨علي ؓ⁩ came back from ⁨صفين⁩ he fitted ⁨الأشتر⁩ out to go and govern Egypt. He died on the road, poisoned; it is said that a slave of ⁨عثمان ؓ⁩'s met him and poisoned honey for him. ⁨علي ؓ⁩ had found him hard work, because he was difficult to handle — and when the death notice came, he said something over him that the Arabs remembered. On the other side, ⁨عمرو بن العاص ؓ⁩ was glad, and made a remark about it that they remembered too.

**The statement:**
> إِنَّا للهِ، مَالِكٌ، وَمَا مَالِكٌ! وَهَلْ مَوْجُوْدٌ مِثْلُ ذَلِكَ؟! لَوْ كَانَ حَدِيْداً لَكَانَ قَيْداً، وَلَوْ كَانَ حَجَراً لَكَانَ صَلْداً، عَلَى مِثْلِهِ فَلْتَبْكِ البَوَاكِي.
> — ⁨سیر أعلام النبلاء ج۴ ص۳۴⁩ · https://shamela.ws/book/10906/3187
> *English:* "To God we belong. Mālik — and what a Mālik! Is the like of that to be found? Had he been iron he would have been a fetter; had he been stone he would have been solid rock. For such a man let the mourning women weep."

**⁨عبرت⁩:** The men hardest to command are mourned hardest when they are gone.

**Hands-up?** no

### E-PG22 · God has armies made of honey

**Tier:** GOOD · **When:** after ⁨صفين⁩ `[SOURCED]` · **Map:** No map.

**What happened:** The news of ⁨الأشتر⁩'s death on the Egyptian road reached the Syrian side, and ⁨عمرو بن العاص ؓ⁩ said one sentence about it. It is quoted here for one reason only: it lets the room hear, in half a line, how completely each side had come to see the other as an obstacle — and it is recorded in a Sunni source without any comment attached to it.

**The statement:**
> وَسُرَّ بِهَلاَكِهِ عَمْرُو بنُ العَاصِ، وَقَالَ: إِنَّ للهِ جُنُوْداً مِنْ عَسَلٍ.
> — ⁨سیر أعلام النبلاء ج۴ ص۳۵⁩ · https://shamela.ws/book/10906/3188
> *English:* "⁨عمرو بن العاص⁩ was pleased at his destruction, and said: God has armies made of honey."

**⁨عبرت⁩:** War teaches men to be glad of a death, and that is one of the things it costs.

**Hands-up?** no

### E-PG23 · Born at the Farewell Pilgrimage

**Tier:** CORE · **When:** born 10 AH, killed 37 AH `[SOURCED]` · **Map:** No map.

**What happened:** ⁨محمد بن أبي بكر ؓ⁩ was born to ⁨أسماء بنت عميس ؓ⁩ during the Farewell Pilgrimage itself, at the moment the pilgrims put on iḥrām. He was the son of ⁨الصديق ؓ⁩, the half-brother of ⁨أم المؤمنين عائشہ ؓ⁩, and the father of ⁨القاسم بن محمد⁩, one of the seven jurists of Medina. He was a child of the Prophet's ﷺ last year and he was dead before he was thirty. That line — ⁨الصديق ؓ⁩, then ⁨محمد⁩, then ⁨القاسم⁩ the jurist — runs straight through the middle of the fitna, and the room should see it whole before it hears anything else about him.

**The statement:**
> وَلَدَتْهُ: أَسْمَاءُ بِنْتُ عُمَيْسٍ فِي حَجَّةِ الوَدَاعِ، وَقْتَ الإِحْرَامِ.
> — ⁨سیر أعلام النبلاء ج۳ ص۴۸۲⁩ · https://shamela.ws/book/10906/3090
> *English:* "⁨أسماء بنت عميس⁩ bore him during the Farewell Pilgrimage, at the time of iḥrām."

**⁨عبرت⁩:** Two generations of one house can stand on opposite sides of the same quarrel and the house still produce a scholar the whole ummah lives by.

**Hands-up?** no

### E-PG24 · Guard me for the sake of ⁨أبو بكر⁩

**Tier:** CORE · **When:** sent to Egypt in ⁨رمضان⁩ 37 AH `[SOURCED]`; the page gives no year for the killing `[CONVENTIONAL-ESTIMATE]` · **Map:** Egypt — the delta and ⁨فسطاط⁩.

**What happened:** ⁨علي ؓ⁩ sent ⁨محمد بن أبي بكر ؓ⁩ to govern Egypt in the Ramadan of the thirty-seventh year. ⁨قيس بن سعد ؓ⁩, whom he was replacing, had already warned him: nephew, be careful of these people — they will hand you over, and the two of you will be killed. ⁨الذهبي⁩ adds four words to that warning: and so it was. When ⁨معاوية ؓ⁩'s army came, his force was scattered and he hid in the house of an Egyptian woman, who gave him up. He was brought before ⁨معاوية بن حديج⁩ and asked for his father's sake, and was answered in the plainest possible terms.

**The statement:**
> فَقَالَ: احْفَظُونِي فِي أَبِي بَكْرٍ. فَقَالَ مُعَاوِيَةُ بنُ حُدَيْجٍ: قَتَلْتَ ثَمَانِيْنَ مِنْ قَوْمِي فِي دَمِ الشَّهِيْدِ عُثْمَانَ، وَأَتْرُكُكَ، وَأَنْتَ صَاحِبُهُ!
> — ⁨سیر أعلام النبلاء ج۳ ص۴۸۲⁩ · https://shamela.ws/book/10906/3090
> *English:* "He said: guard me for the sake of ⁨أبو بكر⁩. ⁨معاوية بن حديج⁩ said: you killed eighty of my people over the blood of the martyr ⁨عثمان⁩ — and shall I let you go, who were his companion in it?"

**⁨عبرت⁩:** In a feud a man's name stops protecting him at exactly the point when he needs it most.

**Hands-up?** no

### E-PG25 · Four governors of Egypt in three years

**Tier:** GOOD · **When:** 36–39 AH `[SOURCED]` · **Map:** Egypt — change the name on the province four times.

**What happened:** Egypt passed through four hands in about three years, and every hand-over was a defeat for someone. ⁨قيس بن سعد ؓ⁩ held it for ⁨علي ؓ⁩ from the thirty-sixth year and was removed in the thirty-seventh, on the strength of a letter he never wrote. The command then went to ⁨الأشتر⁩, who was poisoned on the road and never arrived. It went next to ⁨محمد بن أبي بكر ؓ⁩, who was killed there. And then ⁨عمرو بن العاص ؓ⁩ took the province, and kept it until he died.

**The statement:**
> ثُمَّ عَزَلَهُ، وَوَلَّى الأَشْتَرَ، فَمَاتَ قَبْلَ أَنْ يَصِلَ إِلَيْهَا (١) . قُلْتُ: فَقِيْلَ: سُمَّ. وَوَلَّى مُحَمَّدَ بنَ أَبِي بَكْرٍ، فَقُتِلَ بِهَا، وَغَلَبَ عَلَيْهَا عَمْرٌو.
> — ⁨سیر أعلام النبلاء ج۳ ص۱۰۹⁩ · https://shamela.ws/book/10906/2718
> *English:* "Then he removed him and appointed ⁨الأشتر⁩, who died before reaching it. I say: and it is said he was poisoned. Then he appointed ⁨محمد بن أبي بكر⁩, who was killed there — and ⁨عمرو⁩ took possession of it."

**⁨عبرت⁩:** A province is not governed by whoever is appointed to it, but by whoever survives to arrive.

**Hands-up?** no

### E-PG26 · ⁨المِرقال⁩, the man who pressed forward

**Tier:** CORE · **When:** killed at ⁨صفين⁩, 37 AH `[SOURCED]` · **Map:** ⁨صفين⁩.

**What happened:** ⁨هاشم بن عتبة بن أبي وقاص⁩ was of ⁨بنو زهرة⁩, the Prophet's ﷺ mother's clan, and the nephew of ⁨سعد بن أبي وقاص ؓ⁩, who commanded at ⁨القادسية⁩ and sat in the ⁨شورى⁩. He was born while the Prophet ﷺ was alive; some counted him a Companion for that reason, and ⁨الذهبي⁩ records that they did without making the claim himself. He lost an eye at ⁨اليرموك⁩ and was present at the taking of Damascus. They called him ⁨المِرقال⁩ because he pressed forward at speed in battle. At ⁨صفين⁩ he carried ⁨علي ؓ⁩'s own standard, and he was killed under it.

**The statement:**
> وَكَانَ مَعَهُ رَايَةُ الإِمَامِ عَلِيٍّ يَوْمَ صِفِّيْنَ، فَقُتِلَ يَوْمَئِذٍ (٢) . وَكَانَ مَوْصُوَفاً بِالشَّجَاعَةِ وَالإِقدَامِ - رَحِمَهُ اللهُ تَعَالَى -.
> — ⁨سیر أعلام النبلاء ج۳ ص۴۸۶⁩ · https://shamela.ws/book/10906/3094
> *English:* "The standard of the Imām ⁨علي⁩ was with him on the day of ⁨صفين⁩, and he was killed that day. He was described as a man of courage and of pressing forward — may God have mercy on him."

**⁨عبرت⁩:** A nickname earned on the battlefield outlives the battle it was earned in.

**Hands-up?** no

### E-PG27 · The uncle stayed out, the nephew carried the banner

**Tier:** GOOD · **When:** 37 AH `[SOURCED]` · **Map:** Medina and ⁨صفين⁩ on the same screen.

**What happened:** ⁨سعد بن أبي وقاص ؓ⁩, the conqueror of Iraq and one of the six men of the ⁨شورى⁩, held back from the whole quarrel. His nephew ⁨هاشم بن عتبة⁩ carried ⁨علي ؓ⁩'s standard into ⁨صفين⁩ and died holding it. The two men are of one clan and one household. This single fact does more than any argument to show the room that the division was not tribal, not regional, and not a matter of families lining up behind one side — it ran through the middle of households.

**The statement:**
> مِنْ أُمَرَاءِ عَلِيٍّ يَوْمَ صِفِّيْنَ.
> — ⁨سیر أعلام النبلاء ج۳ ص۴۸۶⁩ · https://shamela.ws/book/10906/3094
> *English:* "One of ⁨علي⁩'s commanders on the day of ⁨صفين⁩."

**⁨عبرت⁩:** When the line of division runs inside a single family, no rule about who was on which side can be drawn from birth.

**Hands-up?** Before telling it, ask the room whether they would expect an uncle and a nephew to take the same side.

---

### E-PG28 · ⁨حُجْر⁩ the good

**Tier:** CORE · **When:** delegation in the Prophet's ﷺ time; killed 51 AH `[SOURCED]` · **Map:** Kufa, then Damascus.

**What happened:** ⁨حجر بن عدي⁩ was of ⁨كندة⁩, the same tribe as ⁨الأشعث بن قيس ؓ⁩, and his own genealogy meets ⁨الأشعث⁩'s three generations up. He came in a delegation to the Prophet ﷺ and narrated nothing from him; what he learned, he learned from ⁨علي ؓ⁩ and from ⁨عمار ؓ⁩. He fought at ⁨القادسية⁩, and it was he who opened the village of ⁨مرج عذراء⁩ outside Damascus. He was a chief, a commander who was obeyed, a man of prayer and fasting, and forward in objecting to what he thought wrong. ⁨الذهبي⁩ gives him one title in the heading and it is not a compliment paid by us: ⁨الشهيد⁩.

**The statement:**
> وَكَانَ شَرِيفاً، أَمِيْراً مُطَاعاً، أَمَّاراً بِالمَعْرُوفِ، مُقْدِماً عَلَى الإِنْكَارِ، مِنْ شِيْعَةِ عَلِيٍّ -رَضِيَ اللهُ عَنْهُمَا -. شَهِدَ صِفِّيْنَ أَمِيْراً، وَكَانَ ذَا صَلاَحٍ وَتَعَبُّدٍ.
> — ⁨سیر أعلام النبلاء ج۳ ص۴۶۳⁩ · https://shamela.ws/book/10906/3071
> *English:* "He was a man of standing, a commander who was obeyed, much given to enjoining the good and forward in objecting; of ⁨علي⁩'s party — may God be pleased with them both. He was present at ⁨صفين⁩ as a commander, and he was a man of piety and devotion."

**⁨عبرت⁩:** A man can be sound in his religion and still be, for his government, a problem it decides to solve.

**Hands-up?** no

---

### E-PG29 · The village he had opened himself

**Tier:** CORE · **When:** 51 AH `[SOURCED]` · **Map:** ⁨مرج عذراء⁩, north-east of Damascus.

**What happened:** ⁨حجر⁩ kept contradicting the governor ⁨زياد⁩ in Kufa, from the floor of the mosque, while he was preaching. ⁨زياد⁩ warned him privately and at length, and he did not stop. ⁨زياد⁩ gathered seventy men to write out testimony against him and sent him to Damascus under guard with a group of his companions. When they reached the village where they were to be killed, ⁨حجر⁩ asked what the place was called. They told him: ⁨عذراء⁩. And he answered with a fact about his own life that he had not expected to need.

**The statement:**
> أَمَا -وَاللهِ - إِنِّيْ لأَوَّلُ مُسْلِمٍ نَبَّحَ كِلاَبَهَا
> — ⁨سیر أعلام النبلاء ج۳ ص۴۶۴⁩ · https://shamela.ws/book/10906/3072
> فِي سَبِيْلِ اللهِ.
> — ⁨سیر أعلام النبلاء ج۳ ص۴۶۵⁩ · https://shamela.ws/book/10906/3073
> *English:* "By God, I am the first Muslim who ever set its dogs barking in the way of God."

**⁨عبرت⁩:** A man may be brought to die in the very place he opened for Islam, and the sources record it without explaining it away.

**Hands-up?** no

---

### E-PG30 · A drawn sword, an unrolled shroud, a dug grave

**Tier:** CORE · **When:** 51 AH `[SOURCED]` · **Map:** ⁨مرج عذراء⁩.

**What happened:** At ⁨عذراء⁩ they were brought out in irons and each man handed to the man who would kill him. ⁨حجر⁩ asked to be allowed to pray two rakʿas first. They let him, and he made them long. Somebody said: you drew it out — have you lost your nerve? Their own clansmen had come with the shrouds and had already dug the graves, and ⁨حجر⁩ could see all of it while he prayed. And when they told him to stretch out his neck, he refused, and gave a reason for refusing.

**The statement:**
> مَا صَلَّيْتُ صَلاَةً أَخَفَّ مِنْهَا، وَلَئِنْ جَزِعْتُ لَقَدْ رَأَيْتُ سَيْفاً مَشْهُوْراً، وَكَفَناً مَنْشُوراً، وَقَبْراً مَحْفُوراً.
> — ⁨سیر أعلام النبلاء ج۳ ص۴۶۵⁩ · https://shamela.ws/book/10906/3073
> *English:* "I have never prayed a lighter prayer than that one. And if I had lost my nerve — I have just seen a drawn sword, an unrolled shroud and a dug grave."

**⁨عبرت⁩:** Fear is not a fault in a man who has been shown, all at once, exactly what is coming.

**Hands-up?** no

---

### E-PG31 · We call upon You against our own community

**Tier:** CORE · **When:** 51 AH `[SOURCED]` · **Map:** Kufa and Damascus, with ⁨عذراء⁩ between them.

**What happened:** ⁨حجر⁩ and his companions were thirteen men. Seven of them were killed before a messenger arrived from ⁨معاوية ؓ⁩ with an order releasing the rest, and six were spared. What ⁨حجر⁩ said before he was killed names both halves of the Muslim world in one sentence, and puts each of them on the same charge-sheet. He had been condemned on the written testimony of the people of Iraq and executed by the people of Syria, and he said so.

**The statement:**
> اللَّهُمَّ إِنَّا نَسْتَعْدِيكَ (٣) عَلَى أُمَّتِنَا، فَإِنَّ أَهْلَ العِرَاقِ شَهِدُوا عَلَيْنَا، وَإِنَّ أَهْلَ الشَّامِ قَتَلُوْنَا.
> — ⁨سیر أعلام النبلاء ج۳ ص۴۶۵⁩ · https://shamela.ws/book/10906/3073
> *English:* "O God, we call upon You against our own community: the people of Iraq testified against us, and the people of Syria killed us."

**⁨عبرت⁩:** When a man's own community stands on both sides of his execution, there is nobody left to appeal to but God.

**Hands-up?** no

---

### E-PG32 · ⁨ابن عمر ؓ⁩ in the market, and ⁨عائشہ ؓ⁩'s messenger

**Tier:** GOOD · **When:** 51 AH `[SOURCED]` · **Map:** Medina.

**What happened:** The news of what had been done at ⁨عذراء⁩ travelled. ⁨عائشہ ؓ⁩ had already sent ⁨عبد الرحمن بن الحارث بن هشام⁩ to ⁨معاوية ؓ⁩ to ask that the men be released; he arrived after they had been killed, and asked ⁨معاوية ؓ⁩ where the forbearance of ⁨أبو سفيان⁩ had gone — and ⁨معاوية ؓ⁩ answered that it had gone with the absence of men like him, which ⁨الذهبي⁩ glosses in four words: meaning that he regretted it. ⁨ابن عمر ؓ⁩ was sitting in the market of Medina when they told him.

**The statement:**
> كَانَ ابْنُ عُمَرَ فِي السُّوقِ، فَنُعِيَ إِلَيْهِ حُجْرٌ، فَأَطْلَقَ (٤) حَبْوَتَهُ، وَقَامَ، وَقَدْ غَلَبَ عَلَيْهِ النَّحِيْبُ.
> — ⁨سیر أعلام النبلاء ج۳ ص۴۶۶⁩ · https://shamela.ws/book/10906/3074
> *English:* "⁨ابن عمر⁩ was in the market when the death of ⁨حجر⁩ was announced to him. He loosened his wrap, stood up, and was overcome with weeping."

**⁨عبرت⁩:** The measure of an act is often best taken from the face of a man who had no part in it.

**Hands-up?** no

### E-PG33 · Two cousins, both called ⁨حُجْر⁩

**Tier:** GOOD · **When:** 37–51 AH `[SOURCED]` · **Map:** Kufa, Damascus, Armenia.

**What happened:** There are two men of ⁨كندة⁩ called ⁨حجر⁩ in this generation and the room will confuse them unless they are separated out loud. The first is ⁨حجر بن عدي⁩, called ⁨حُجْر⁩ the good, executed at ⁨عذراء⁩. The second is his paternal cousin ⁨حجر بن يزيد بن سلمة⁩, called ⁨حُجْر⁩ the bad — a different man, who came in a delegation to the Prophet ﷺ, was of ⁨علي ؓ⁩'s party, was present at the day of the two arbitrators, and afterwards became one of ⁨معاوية ؓ⁩'s governors, over Armenia. Neither of them narrated anything. Two cousins of one clan: the state killed one and appointed the other.

**The statement:**
> فَهُوَ ابْنُ عَمٍّ لِحُجْرِ الخَيْرِ ... وَفَدَ إِلَى النَّبِيِّ -صَلَّى اللَّهُ عَلَيْهِ وَسَلَّمَ - ثُمَّ كَانَ مِنْ شِيْعَةِ عَلِيٍّ، وَشَهِدَ يَوْمَ الحَكَمَينِ، ثُمَّ صَارَ مِنْ أُمَرَاءِ مُعَاوِيَةَ، فَوَلاَّهُ أَرْمِيْنِيَةَ.
> — ⁨سیر أعلام النبلاء ج۳ ص۴۶۷⁩ · https://shamela.ws/book/10906/3075
> *English:* "He is a paternal cousin of ⁨حجر⁩ the good ... He came in a delegation to the Prophet ﷺ, was then of ⁨علي⁩'s party, was present at the day of the two arbitrators, and then became one of ⁨معاوية⁩'s governors, and was given Armenia."

**⁨عبرت⁩:** Two men of the same house and the same beginning can end in entirely different places, and history keeps both of them.

**Hands-up?** no

### E-PG34 · Three brothers and one banner

**Tier:** CORE · **When:** ⁨الجمل⁩, 36 AH `[SOURCED]` · **Map:** Basra.

**What happened:** ⁨صعصعة بن صوحان⁩ was one of the orators of the Arabs and one of the senior men around ⁨علي ؓ⁩. ⁨ابن سعد⁩ declared him trustworthy; ⁨الشعبي⁩ and others narrated from him; he was a chief, obeyed, a commander and a very fine speaker. On the day of ⁨الجمل⁩ his two brothers were killed, and he picked up the standard they had been carrying. He lived on into the caliphate of ⁨معاوية ؓ⁩.

**The statement:**
> أَحَدُ خُطَبَاءِ العَرَبِ. كَانَ مِنْ كِبَارِ أَصْحَابِ عَلِيٍّ. قُتِلَ
> — ⁨سیر أعلام النبلاء ج۳ ص۵۲۸⁩ · https://shamela.ws/book/10906/3136
> أَخَوَاهُ يَوْمَ الجَمَلِ، فَأَخَذَ صَعْصَعَةُ الرَّايَةَ.
> — ⁨سیر أعلام النبلاء ج۳ ص۵۲۹⁩ · https://shamela.ws/book/10906/3137
> *English:* "One of the orators of the Arabs. He was among the senior of ⁨علي⁩'s companions. His two brothers were killed on the day of ⁨الجمل⁩, and ⁨صعصعة⁩ took up the standard."

**⁨عبرت⁩:** A civil war is counted in households before it is counted in armies.

**Hands-up?** no

### E-PG35 · Wash no blood off me

**Tier:** GOOD · **When:** ⁨الجمل⁩, 36 AH `[SOURCED]` · **Map:** Basra.

**What happened:** ⁨زيد بن صوحان⁩, ⁨صعصعة⁩'s brother, was dying at ⁨الجمل⁩, and he left instructions about his own burial. They are exact and they are strange, and the reason he gives for them is stranger: he means to arrive on the Day of Resurrection as a man with a case to argue, and he wants to arrive dressed for it. A second wording of the same instruction has him ask to be buried in one grave with his brother. Ten years later, ⁨حجر بن عدي⁩ used the same word about himself, on the night before he was killed at ⁨عذراء⁩.

**The statement:**
> لاَ تَغْسِلُوا عَنِّي دَماً، وَلاَ تَنْزِعُوا عَنِّي ثَوْباً، إِلاَّ الخُفَّيْنِ، وَأَرْمِسُونِي فِي الأَرْضِ رَمْساً، فَإِنِّي مُخَاصِمٌ، أُحَاجُّ يَوْمَ القِيَامَةِ.
> — ⁨سیر أعلام النبلاء ج۳ ص۵۲۸⁩ · https://shamela.ws/book/10906/3136
> *English:* "Do not wash any blood off me, and do not take any garment off me except the two leather socks; and press me into the earth. For I am a litigant: I shall plead on the Day of Resurrection."

**⁨عبرت⁩:** Men who fought each other in this life both expected to be heard in the next, and neither of them expected to be the judge.

**Hands-up?** no

### E-PG36 · The best of the men of Yemen

**Tier:** CORE · **When:** came 10 AH; died 51 or 54 AH `[SOURCED]` · **Map:** Yemen, then Kufa, then ⁨قرقيسياء⁩ on the Khābūr.

**What happened:** ⁨جرير بن عبد الله البجلي ؓ⁩ came to Medina in the Ramadan of the tenth year with a hundred and fifty of his own people. He was a very tall, very handsome man, and among the last of the Companions to become Muslim. He destroyed the idol-house of ⁨ذو الخلصة⁩, which they used to call the Yemeni Kaʿba. He commanded the right wing of ⁨سعد بن أبي وقاص ؓ⁩ at ⁨القادسية⁩, governed ⁨همذان⁩ for ⁨عثمان ؓ⁩ and lost an eye there, and carried ⁨علي ؓ⁩'s message to ⁨معاوية ؓ⁩. The Prophet ﷺ had announced him before he ever walked in.

**The statement:**
> (إِنَّهُ سَيَدْخُلُ عَلَيْكُمْ مِنْ هَذَا الفَجِّ مِنْ خَيْرِ ذِي يَمَنٍ، أَلاَ وَإِنَّ عَلَى وَجْهِهِ مِسْحَةَ مَلَكٍ)
> — ⁨سیر أعلام النبلاء ج۲ ص۵۳۱⁩ · https://shamela.ws/book/10906/2511
> *English:* "There will come in upon you from this mountain pass one of the best of the men of Yemen — and on his face is the touch of an angel."

**⁨عبرت⁩:** A man who arrives late can still be announced ahead of himself.

**Hands-up?** no

---

### E-PG37 · ⁨عمر ؓ⁩, ⁨جرير ؓ⁩, and the whole congregation

**Tier:** GOOD · **When:** in ⁨عمر ؓ⁩'s caliphate `[SOURCED]` · **Map:** No map.

**What happened:** ⁨جرير ؓ⁩ was sitting with ⁨عمر ؓ⁩ when someone in the gathering broke wind. ⁨عمر ؓ⁩ required the man responsible to get up and make wuḍūʾ. Nobody moved, and the man would have been exposed by his own movement. ⁨جرير ؓ⁩ said one sentence, and ⁨عمر ؓ⁩ took it at once. Some of the narrators add what ⁨عمر ؓ⁩ said to him afterwards: may God have mercy on you — what a chief you were in the Jāhiliyya, and what a chief you have been in Islam.

**The statement:**
> فَقَالَ جَرِيْرٌ: اعْزِمْ عَلَيْنَا جَمِيْعاً. فَقَالَ: عَزَمْتُ عَلَيَّ وَعَلَيْكُمْ لَمَا قُمْنَا، فَتَوَضَّأْنَا، ثُمَّ صَلَّيْنَا.
> — ⁨سیر أعلام النبلاء ج۲ ص۵۳۵⁩ · https://shamela.ws/book/10906/2515
> *English:* "⁨جرير⁩ said: require it of all of us. So he said: I require it of myself and of you all — that we rise, make wuḍūʾ, and then pray."

**⁨عبرت⁩:** The quickest way to cover a man is to put yourself under the same rule he is under.

**Hands-up?** Before telling it, ask: how would you save someone from being shamed in front of a room without saying his name?

---

### E-PG38 · I do not fight a man who says there is no god but God

**Tier:** CORE · **When:** 37 AH or after `[SOURCED]`, with a weak chain — say so · **Map:** ⁨قرقيسياء⁩ on the Khābūr, between Iraq and Syria.

**What happened:** ⁨جرير ؓ⁩ carried ⁨علي ؓ⁩'s demand for allegiance to ⁨معاوية ؓ⁩, and afterwards he stepped out of the whole quarrel and lived at ⁨قرقيسياء⁩, holding aloof from both men until he died. ⁨علي ؓ⁩ sent ⁨ابن عباس ؓ⁩ and ⁨الأشعث بن قيس ؓ⁩ to him there with a message: what you did in parting from ⁨معاوية⁩ was well seen, and I put you in the place the Messenger of God ﷺ put you in. And ⁨جرير ؓ⁩ gave them his reason — the Yemen expedition he had been sent on in the Prophet's ﷺ own lifetime, and what he had been told to do when the people said the words.

**The statement:**
> إِنَّ رَسُوْلَ اللهِ -صَلَّى اللَّهُ عَلَيْهِ وَسَلَّمَ- بَعَثَنِي إِلَى اليَمَنِ، أُقَاتِلُهُمْ حَتَّى يَقُوْلُوا: لاَ إِلَهَ إِلاَّ اللهُ، فَإِذَا قَالُوا، حَرُمَتْ دِمَاؤُهُمْ وَأَمْوَالُهُمْ، فَلاَ أُقَاتِلُ مَنْ يَقُوْلُ: لاَ إِلَهَ إِلاَّ اللهُ.
> — ⁨سیر أعلام النبلاء ج۲ ص۵۳۶⁩ · https://shamela.ws/book/10906/2516
> *English:* "The Messenger of God ﷺ sent me to Yemen to fight them until they should say *there is no god but God*; and when they said it, their blood and their property became inviolable. So I do not fight a man who says *there is no god but God*."

**⁨عبرت⁩:** The reason a man gives for standing aside can be the same command he was once given for going forward.

**Hands-up?** no

**Note for the speaker, not for the slide:** the printed edition marks this chain as weak — ⁨أبان بن عبد الله⁩ is soft in memory, and ⁨إبراهيم بن جرير⁩ did not hear from his father. Introduce it as *what ⁨سیر⁩ records ⁨جرير ؓ⁩ giving as his reason*, and do not present it as established.

---

### E-PG39 · The name his real name lost

**Tier:** CORE · **When:** delegation in the Prophet's ﷺ time; died 40 AH `[SOURCED]` · **Map:** ⁨حضرموت⁩, then Medina, then Kufa.

**What happened:** ⁨الأشعث بن قيس ؓ⁩ was of ⁨كندة⁩, the same tribe as ⁨حجر بن عدي⁩, and came to the Prophet ﷺ at the head of seventy of his people. His real name was ⁨معدي كرب⁩; he had permanently unkempt hair, and the nickname ⁨الأشعث⁩, the dishevelled, took the name over entirely. A verse of ⁨سورة آل عمران⁩ was revealed over a case of his, and he said so himself. He lost an eye at ⁨اليرموك⁩. He governed ⁨أذربيجان⁩ for ⁨عثمان ؓ⁩, and at ⁨صفين⁩ he was the most senior commander ⁨علي ؓ⁩ had.

**The statement:**
> وَكَانَ اسْمُ الأَشْعَثِ: مَعْدِيْ كَرِبَ. وَكَانَ أَبَداً أَشْعَثَ الرَّأْسِ؛ فَغَلَبَ عَلَيْهِ.
> — ⁨سیر أعلام النبلاء ج۲ ص۳۸⁩ · https://shamela.ws/book/10906/2018
> *English:* "⁨الأشعث⁩'s name was ⁨معدي كرب⁩. His head was perpetually dishevelled, and so the name took him over."

**⁨عبرت⁩:** The name people actually use for a man tells you what they actually noticed about him.

**Hands-up?** no

---

### E-PG40 · The wedding feast in the camel market

**Tier:** CORE · **When:** 11–12 AH `[SOURCED]` · **Map:** Medina.

**What happened:** ⁨الأشعث ؓ⁩ had apostatised in the ⁨ردة⁩ with a number of ⁨كندة⁩. He was besieged, and he took safe conduct — for seventy men, and not for himself. He was brought captive to ⁨أبو بكر ؓ⁩, who told him plainly: we are going to kill you, you have no safe conduct. And he said: will you show me favour, and I become Muslim? ⁨أبو بكر ؓ⁩ did, and loosed his bonds, and married his own sister to him. So ⁨الأشعث ؓ⁩ drew his sword, walked into the camel market of Medina, and began hamstringing every camel and every bull he saw, while the people shouted that he had apostatised again. Then he threw the sword down and explained himself.

**The statement:**
> وَاللهِ مَا كَفَرْتُ؛ وَلَكِنَّ هَذَا الرَّجُلَ زَوَّجَنِي أُخْتَهُ؛ وَلَو كُنَّا فِي بِلاَدِنَا لَكَانَتْ لَنَا وَلِيْمَةٌ غَيْرُ هَذِهِ، يَا أَهْلَ المَدِيْنَةِ، انْحَرُوا، وَكُلُوا! وَيَا أَهْلَ الإِبِلِ تَعَالَوْا خُذُوا شَرْوَاهَا!
> — ⁨سیر أعلام النبلاء ج۲ ص۳۹⁩ · https://shamela.ws/book/10906/2019
> *English:* "By God, I have not disbelieved. This man has married his sister to me, and had we been in our own country we would have had a wedding feast other than this one. People of Medina — slaughter and eat! And owners of the camels, come and take the like of them!"

**⁨عبرت⁩:** A man newly forgiven will sometimes announce it in the loudest and least convenient way available to him.

**Hands-up?** no

---

### E-PG41 · This man did not apostatise, and I did

**Tier:** CORE · **When:** after 12 AH `[SOURCED]` · **Map:** No map.

**What happened:** At a funeral where both men were standing, ⁨الأشعث بن قيس ؓ⁩ stepped back and put ⁨جرير بن عبد الله ؓ⁩ forward to lead the prayer. Somebody asked him why, and he gave the reason in six words. He had been forgiven, married into ⁨الصديق ؓ⁩'s own family, and made a commander; and he still said this about himself in public, years afterwards.

**The statement:**
> إِنَّ هَذَا لَمْ يَرْتَدَّ، وَإِنِّي ارْتَدَدْتُ.
> — ⁨سیر أعلام النبلاء ج۲ ص۴۰⁩ · https://shamela.ws/book/10906/2020
> *English:* "This man did not apostatise. And I did."

**⁨عبرت⁩:** A man who will say the worst thing about himself out loud has taken its power away from everybody else.

**Hands-up?** no
