### E-PG42 · God, God, in the Umma of Muhammad ﷺ

**Tier:** CORE · **When:** ⁨صفين⁩, 37 AH `[SOURCED]` · **Map:** the Euphrates at ⁨صفين⁩ — mark the water.

**What happened:** ⁨معاوية ؓ⁩'s men reached the Euphrates first and cut the Iraqis off from the water. ⁨علي ؓ⁩ sent ⁨الأشعث ؓ⁩ with two thousand against ⁨أبو الأعور⁩'s five thousand and took the water back. But before that, a Syrian called ⁨أبو الصلت الحضرمي⁩ tells it from his own side of the line: a single horseman rode up, uncovered his face, and it was ⁨الأشعث بن قيس⁩. He did not challenge anyone. He put a question to ⁨معاوية ؓ⁩ twice over, once in each direction, and then he recited a verse of ⁨سورة الحجرات⁩. ⁨معاوية ؓ⁩ asked him what he wanted. He said: let us at the water. And ⁨معاوية ؓ⁩ turned to his commander and said: let our brothers at the water.

**The statement:**
> اللهَ اللهَ يَا مُعَاوِيَةُ فِي أُمَّةِ مُحَمَّدٍ -صَلَّى اللَّهُ عَلَيْهِ وَسَلَّمَ-! هَبُوْا أَنَّكُمْ قَتَلْتُم أَهْلَ العِرَاقِ، فَمَنْ لِلْبُعُوْثِ وَالذَّرَارِي؟ أَمْ هَبُوْا أَنَّا قَتَلْنَاكُم، فَمَنْ لِلْبُعُوْثِ وَالذَّرَارِي؟ إِنَّ اللهَ يَقُوْلُ: {وَإِنْ طَائِفَتَانِ مِنَ المُؤْمِنِيْنَ اقْتَتَلُوا، فَأَصْلِحُوا بَيْنَهُمَا}
> — ⁨سیر أعلام النبلاء ج۲ ص۴۱⁩ · https://shamela.ws/book/10906/2021
> *English:* "God, God, O ⁨معاوية⁩, in the matter of the Umma of Muḥammad ﷺ! Suppose you kill the people of Iraq: who is left for the frontier expeditions and the children? Or suppose we kill you: who is left for the frontier expeditions and the children? God says: and if two parties of the believers fight, then make peace between them."

**⁨عبرت⁩:** Both armies knew the same verse, and on that day it was enough to settle one thing between them.

**Hands-up?** Before telling it, ask: what is the one book both sides in that valley could be held to?

---

### E-PG43 · Where will you find an imām like ⁨علي⁩?

**Tier:** GOOD · **When:** 36–40 AH `[SOURCED]` · **Map:** No map.

**What happened:** ⁨الأشعث ؓ⁩ used to warn people against the fitan. Someone put the obvious objection to him: you went out with ⁨علي⁩ yourself. And he answered it in five words. ⁨البخاري⁩ records it in the ⁨التاريخ الكبير⁩. It is a complete answer, it names nobody else, and it concedes nothing about anyone on the other side.

**The statement:**
> حَذَّرَ الأَشْعَثُ مِنَ الفِتَنِ، فَقِيْلَ لَهُ: خَرَجْتَ مَعَ عَلِيٍّ! فَقَالَ: وَمَنْ لَكَ إِمَامٌ مِثْلُ عَلِيٍّ!
> — ⁨سیر أعلام النبلاء ج۲ ص۴۰⁩ · https://shamela.ws/book/10906/2020
> *English:* "⁨الأشعث⁩ warned against the fitan, and was told: you went out with ⁨علي⁩! He said: and where will you find yourself an imām like ⁨علي⁩?"

**⁨عبرت⁩:** Warning against a thing and taking part in it can both be honest, if the man will say why.

**Hands-up?** no

---

### E-PG44 · Three generations of one house

**Tier:** GOOD · **When:** 12–40 AH `[SOURCED]`; the years of the grandson's rising are not on the page `[CONVENTIONAL-ESTIMATE]` · **Map:** Medina, ⁨صفين⁩, then Iraq and Sijistān.

**What happened:** ⁨الأشعث ؓ⁩ died in the fortieth year, and some add: forty nights after ⁨علي ؓ⁩. He was buried in his own house, and ⁨الحسن بن علي ؓ⁩ — whose wife was ⁨الأشعث ؓ⁩'s daughter — came and prayed over him. His son ⁨محمد بن الأشعث⁩ became one of the great commanders. His grandson ⁨عبد الرحمن بن محمد بن الأشعث⁩ led the rising against ⁨الحجاج⁩, in which, it is said, eighty-one pitched battles were fought, most of them going against ⁨الحجاج⁩, before he was abandoned, routed and destroyed. One family: the ⁨ردة⁩, ⁨صفين⁩, and the longest war of the Umayyad century.

**The statement:**
> قَالُوا: تُوُفِّيَ سَنَةَ أَرْبَعِيْنَ. وَزَادَ بَعْضُهُمْ: بَعْدَ عَلِيٍّ -رَضِيَ اللهُ عَنْهُ- بِأَرْبَعِيْنَ لَيْلَةً. وَدُفِنَ فِي دَارِهِ.
> — ⁨سیر أعلام النبلاء ج۲ ص۴۲⁩ · https://shamela.ws/book/10906/2022
> *English:* "They said: he died in the year forty. And some added: forty nights after ⁨علي ؓ⁩. And he was buried in his own house."

**⁨عبرت⁩:** Choices made by one generation are still being fought over by the third.

**Hands-up?** no

---

### E-PG45 · The keeper of the secret

**Tier:** CORE · **When:** 3–36 AH `[SOURCED]` · **Map:** Medina, then ⁨المدائن⁩ on the Tigris.

**What happened:** ⁨حذيفة بن اليمان ؓ⁩ was of ⁨عبس⁩, but his family lived in Medina as allies of the Anṣār, and ⁨الذهبي⁩ counts him among the chief of the Muhājirūn. The Prophet ﷺ had confided to him the names of the hypocrites, which is why the tradition calls him the keeper of the secret. He was the man sent out alone into the dark on the night of ⁨الأحزاب⁩ to bring back news of the enemy. ⁨عمر ؓ⁩ made him governor of ⁨المدائن⁩. And ⁨عمر ؓ⁩ once asked him — the Commander of the Faithful asking the man who had the list — whether he was on it.

**The statement:**
> وَقَدْ نَاشَدَهُ عُمَرُ: أَأَنَا مِنَ المُنَافِقِيْنَ؟ فَقَالَ: لاَ، وَلاَ أُزَكِّي أَحَداً بَعْدَكَ.
> — ⁨سیر أعلام النبلاء ج۲ ص۳۶۴⁩ · https://shamela.ws/book/10906/2344
> *English:* "⁨عمر⁩ adjured him: am I one of the hypocrites? He said: no — and I clear no one after you."

**⁨عبرت⁩:** The man most sure of his own religion was the one who asked whether he was on the list.

**Hands-up?** Before telling it, ask: if a man existed who knew the answer, would you want to ask him?

---

### E-PG46 · He asked about the evil

**Tier:** CORE · **When:** in the Prophet's ﷺ lifetime `[SOURCED]` · **Map:** No map.

**What happened:** This is the sentence the whole of session three can hang on, and it is in ⁨البخاري⁩. Everybody else, ⁨حذيفة ؓ⁩ says, used to ask the Prophet ﷺ about the good; he made a point of asking about the evil, and he gives his reason for it in four words. The long form of the ḥadīth goes on: is there evil after this good? Yes. Is there good after that evil? Yes, and in it is a murkiness. And at the end of it, when he asks what to do if there is no united body and no imām, he is told to keep away from all of those groups, even if he has to bite on the root of a tree until death finds him.

**The statement:**
> كَانَ النَّاسُ يَسْأَلُوْنَ رَسُوْلَ اللهِ -صَلَّى اللَّهُ عَلَيْهِ وَسَلَّمَ- عَنِ الخَيْرِ، وَكُنْتُ أَسْأَلُهُ عَنِ الشَّرِّ، مَخَافَةَ أَنْ يُدْرِكَنِي.
> — ⁨سیر أعلام النبلاء ج۲ ص۳۶۵⁩ · https://shamela.ws/book/10906/2345
> *English:* "People used to ask the Messenger of God ﷺ about the good; and I used to ask him about the evil, for fear that it would overtake me."

**⁨عبرت⁩:** Knowing what is coming is not the same as being able to stop it, and it was still worth asking.

**Hands-up?** no

---

### E-PG47 · ⁨عمر ؓ⁩ hides beside the road

**Tier:** CORE · **When:** in ⁨عمر ؓ⁩'s caliphate `[SOURCED]` · **Map:** ⁨المدائن⁩ on the Tigris, and the road back to Medina.

**What happened:** ⁨عمر ؓ⁩ sent ⁨حذيفة ؓ⁩ to govern ⁨المدائن⁩ and wrote in his commission: hear him, obey him, and give him whatever he asks you for. ⁨حذيفة ؓ⁩ rode out of Medina on a saddled donkey with his food under him, and when the Persian district chiefs came out to receive their new governor, he was holding a loaf of bread and a bone with meat on it. Asked what he wanted from them, he said: food that I can eat, and straw for this donkey of mine, as long as I am among you. Some years later ⁨عمر ؓ⁩ wrote to him to come back — and then hid himself beside the road to see what the governorship had made of him. He saw him arriving in exactly the state he had left in, and came out and took hold of him.

**The statement:**
> أَنْتَ أَخِي، وَأَنَا أَخُوْكَ.
> — ⁨سیر أعلام النبلاء ج۲ ص۳۶۶⁩ · https://shamela.ws/book/10906/2346
> *English:* "You are my brother, and I am yours."

**⁨عبرت⁩:** An office is audited by what it did not change about the man holding it.

**Hands-up?** no

---

### E-PG48 · Forty nights

**Tier:** CORE · **When:** 36 AH `[SOURCED]` · **Map:** ⁨المدائن⁩, with Medina on the same screen.

**What happened:** ⁨حذيفة ؓ⁩ was still governing ⁨المدائن⁩ when ⁨عثمان ؓ⁩ was killed. ⁨جُزَيّ بن بكير⁩ says: when ⁨عثمان⁩ was killed we fled in alarm to ⁨حذيفة⁩, and went in to him. He had forty nights left. The man who had spent his life asking what was coming died before ⁨الجمل⁩ was fought, and what he said as it came is the closing line of the whole evening. He asked for two white garments and refused a robe worth three hundred, saying he would not be left in them long.

**The statement:**
> حَبِيْبٌ جَاءَ عَلَى فَاقَةٍ؛ لاَ أَفْلَحَ مَنْ نَدِمَ! أَلَيْسَ بَعْدِي مَا أَعْلَمُ! الحَمْدُ للهِ الَّذِي سَبَقَ بِي الفِتْنَةَ! قَادَتَهَا وَعُلُوْجَهَا.
> — ⁨سیر أعلام النبلاء ج۲ ص۳۶۸⁩ · https://shamela.ws/book/10906/2348
> *English:* "A beloved one has come at a time of need. He does not prosper who regrets! Is there not after me what I know of? Praise be to God, who has taken me on ahead of the fitna — its leaders and its brutes."

**⁨عبرت⁩:** He thanked God for the one thing nobody else in the story was given: an ending before the worst of it.

**Hands-up?** no

---

### E-PG49 · The blood-money he would not take

**Tier:** GOOD · **When:** ⁨أحد⁩, 3 AH `[SOURCED]` · **Map:** ⁨أحد⁩, outside Medina.

**What happened:** At ⁨أحد⁩, in the confusion after the rout, Muslims fell on ⁨حذيفة ؓ⁩'s own father ⁨اليمان⁩ without recognising him. ⁨حذيفة ؓ⁩ was shouting: my father, my father, people! — and they did not hear him until he was dead. They told him afterwards that by God they had not known him, and they were telling the truth. And ⁨حذيفة ؓ⁩ gave the blood-money away.

**The statement:**
> وَلَمَّا شَدُّوْا عَلَى اليَمَانِ يَوْمَئِذٍ، بَقِيَ حُذَيْفَةُ يَصِيْحُ: أَبِي! أَبِي! يَا قَوْمُ! فَرَاحَ خَطَأً، فَتَصَدَّقَ حُذَيْفَةُ عَلَيْهِم بِدِيَتِهِ (٢) .
> — ⁨سیر أعلام النبلاء ج۲ ص۳۶۲⁩ · https://shamela.ws/book/10906/2342
> *English:* "When they bore down on ⁨اليمان⁩ that day, ⁨حذيفة⁩ was left crying out: my father! my father! People! — and he was gone, by mistake. And ⁨حذيفة⁩ gave them his blood-money as charity."

**⁨عبرت⁩:** The first man in this story to be killed by mistake by his own side was forgiven by his son on the spot, and that is the pattern the later generation could not manage.

**Hands-up?** no

---

### E-PG50 · The son of the man of ⁨سقيفہ⁩

**Tier:** CORE · **When:** 36 AH to the end of ⁨معاوية ؓ⁩'s caliphate — no year of death is given `[SOURCED]` · **Map:** Medina, then Egypt.

**What happened:** ⁨قيس بن سعد بن عبادة ؓ⁩ was chief of the Khazraj and the son of their chief — and his father ⁨سعد بن عبادة ؓ⁩ is the man the room already met in session one, at the roofed hall of ⁨بني ساعدة⁩. ⁨أنس ؓ⁩ says ⁨قيس ؓ⁩ stood to the Prophet ﷺ as the chief of police stands to the commander. He was enormous, and beardless; when he rode a donkey his feet drew lines in the ground. He was famous for two things above all others, and both of them matter for what he did later: open-handedness, and a mind that ⁨الزهري⁩ counted among the five shrewdest in the whole of the fitna.

**The statement:**
> الأَمِيْرُ، المُجَاهِدُ، أَبُو عَبْدِ اللهِ، سَيِّدُ الخَزْرَجِ وَابْنُ سَيِّدِهِم أَبِي ثَابِتٍ، الأَنْصَارِيُّ، الخَزْرَجِيُّ، السَّاعِدِيُّ، صَاحِبُ رَسُوْلِ اللهِ -صَلَّى اللَّهُ عَلَيْهِ وَسَلَّمَ - وَابْنُ صَاحِبِهِ.
> — ⁨سیر أعلام النبلاء ج۳ ص۱۰۲⁩ · https://shamela.ws/book/10906/2711
> *English:* "The commander, the fighter in God's way, ⁨أبو عبد الله⁩, chief of the Khazraj and son of their chief ⁨أبو ثابت⁩, the Anṣārī, the Khazrajī, the Sāʿidī, Companion of the Messenger of God ﷺ and son of his Companion."

**⁨عبرت⁩:** The son of the man of ⁨سقيفہ⁩ served under the caliphs who came after it and held Egypt for one of them.

**Hands-up?** no

---

### E-PG51 · A letter he never wrote

**Tier:** CORE · **When:** 37 AH `[SOURCED]` · **Map:** Egypt and Damascus.

**What happened:** ⁨قيس ؓ⁩ governed Egypt for ⁨علي ؓ⁩ and held it firm. He paid the stipends generously, kept ⁨معاوية ؓ⁩ and ⁨عمرو ؓ⁩ out by manoeuvre rather than by fighting, and sent no grain to Syria. Unable to move him, ⁨معاوية ؓ⁩ wrote a letter as though it came from ⁨قيس⁩ himself, full of grief over ⁨عثمان ؓ⁩ and of obedience. Then he called the people to prayer in Damascus, preached, had the forged letter read out, and told them that ⁨قيس بن سعد⁩, the fang of the Arabs, had seen the matter clearly and had ordered grain sent to them — so pray for ⁨قيس⁩, and raise your hands. They raised their hands. And then ⁨معاوية ؓ⁩ turned to ⁨عمرو ؓ⁩ and told him exactly what would happen next.

**The statement:**
> تَحَيَّنْ خُرُوْجَ العُيُوْنِ، فَفِي سَبْعٍ أَوْ ثَمَانٍ يَصِلُ الخَبَرُ إِلَى عَلِيٍّ، فَيَعْزِلُ قَيْساً، وَكُلُّ مَنْ وَلَّى مِصْرَ كَانَ أَهْوَنَ عَلَيْنَا.
> — ⁨سیر أعلام النبلاء ج۳ ص۱۰۹⁩ · https://shamela.ws/book/10906/2718
> *English:* "Watch for the spies to go out. In seven or eight days the news will reach ⁨علي⁩, and he will remove ⁨قيس⁩ — and anyone else who governs Egypt will be easier for us."

**⁨عبرت⁩:** The most damaging thing done to a governor in that war was not done by an army.

**Hands-up?** no

---

### E-PG52 · I shall not leave him because he removed me

**Tier:** CORE · **When:** 37 AH `[SOURCED]` · **Map:** Medina, then ⁨علي ؓ⁩'s camp.

**What happened:** ⁨قيس ؓ⁩ was dismissed from Egypt and came back to Medina, where ⁨مروان⁩ and ⁨الأسود بن أبي البختري⁩ took counsel to fall on him by night. Word of it reached him. He had every reason to be finished with ⁨علي ؓ⁩, and every reason to look for safety on the other side. Instead he said one sentence and rode out. When he reached ⁨علي ؓ⁩ he explained what he had actually been holding off in Egypt and how; ⁨علي ؓ⁩ understood what he had been doing, took his advice on everything from then on, and put him in command of the vanguard of the army.

**The statement:**
> وَاللهِ إِنَّ هَذَا لَقَبِيْحٌ (٢) أَنْ أُفَارِقَ عَلِيّاً وَإِنْ عَزَلَنِي، وَاللهِ لأَلْحَقَنَّ بِهِ.
> — ⁨سیر أعلام النبلاء ج۳ ص۱۱۰⁩ · https://shamela.ws/book/10906/2719
> *English:* "By God, this is an ugly thing — that I should part from ⁨علي⁩ merely because he removed me. By God, I shall join him."

**⁨عبرت⁩:** Loyalty that survives being dismissed is the only kind that was ever worth anything.

**Hands-up?** Before telling it, ask: what does a man usually do when the leader he served takes his post away on a false report?

---

### E-PG53 · Worse than a hundred thousand men

**Tier:** GOOD · **When:** 37 AH `[SOURCED]` · **Map:** Damascus.

**What happened:** When ⁨معاوية ؓ⁩ heard that ⁨مروان⁩ and ⁨الأسود⁩ had driven ⁨قيس بن سعد ؓ⁩ out of Medina and straight back into ⁨علي ؓ⁩'s camp, he wrote to them, and the letter is the best single estimate of ⁨قيس ؓ⁩ in the sources — given by the man on the other side.

**The statement:**
> أَمْدَدْتُمَا (٥) عَلِيّاً بِقَيْسٍ؟ وَاللهِ لَوْ أَمْدَدْتُمَاهُ بِمائَةِ أَلْفِ مُقَاتِلٍ، مَا كَانَ بِأَغْيَظَ عَلَيَّ مِنْ إِخْرَاجِكُمَا قَيْساً إِلَيْهِ.
> — ⁨سیر أعلام النبلاء ج۳ ص۱۱۰⁩ · https://shamela.ws/book/10906/2719
> *English:* "You have reinforced ⁨علي⁩ with ⁨قيس⁩? By God, had you reinforced him with a hundred thousand fighting men, it would not have galled me more than your driving ⁨قيس⁩ out to him."

**⁨عبرت⁩:** Pushing a capable man out of your reach is a way of handing him to whoever is left.

**Hands-up?** no

---

### E-PG54 · Had I not heard the Prophet ﷺ say it

**Tier:** GOOD · **When:** in ⁨معاوية ؓ⁩'s caliphate `[SOURCED]` · **Map:** No map.

**What happened:** ⁨الزهري⁩ counted ⁨قيس ؓ⁩ among the five shrewdest heads of the fitna. Three separate reports have him saying, in three different wordings, what he could have done if his religion had allowed it. The first is the one to use, because he names the reason: a ḥadīth he had heard from the Prophet ﷺ himself.

**The statement:**
> لَوْلاَ أَنِّي سَمِعْتُ رَسُوْلَ اللهِ -صَلَّى اللَّهُ عَلَيْهِ وَسَلَّمَ - يَقُوْلُ: (المَكْرُ وَالخَدِيْعَةُ فِي النَّارِ) (٤)
> — ⁨سیر أعلام النبلاء ج۳ ص۱۰۷⁩ · https://shamela.ws/book/10906/2716
> لَكُنْتُ مِنْ أَمْكَرِ هَذِهِ الأُمَّةِ.
> — ⁨سیر أعلام النبلاء ج۳ ص۱۰۸⁩ · https://shamela.ws/book/10906/2717
> *English:* "Had I not heard the Messenger of God ﷺ say, stratagem and deceit are in the Fire, I would have been among the craftiest of this Umma."

**⁨عبرت⁩:** The ability a man refuses to use is part of his record too.

**Hands-up?** no

---

### E-PG55 · Safe conduct for five thousand, and none for himself

**Tier:** CORE · **When:** after the killing of ⁨علي ؓ⁩, at ⁨الحسن ؓ⁩'s settlement `[SOURCED]`; the year 41 AH `[CONVENTIONAL-ESTIMATE]` · **Map:** the road from Kufa to Medina, ending at ⁨صِرار⁩.

**What happened:** After ⁨علي ؓ⁩ was killed, ⁨قيس ؓ⁩ was still at the head of the vanguard with five thousand men who had shaved their heads. When ⁨الحسن ؓ⁩ entered into the settlement with ⁨معاوية ؓ⁩, ⁨قيس ؓ⁩ would not enter it, and he put the choice to his own men: if you wish, I will fight alongside you for ever, until whichever of us is quicker dies; and if you wish, I will get safe conduct for you. They said: get it for us. So he got it — and took none for himself. Thirty years earlier ⁨الأشعث بن قيس ؓ⁩ had done exactly the same thing at the end of the ⁨ردة⁩, taking safe conduct for seventy and none for himself. On the road home ⁨قيس ؓ⁩ slaughtered a camel for his men every single day, as far as ⁨صِرار⁩, three miles outside Medina.

**The statement:**
> فَأَخَذَ لَهُم، وَلَمْ يَأْخُذْ لِنَفْسِهِ خَاصَّةً.
> — ⁨سیر أعلام النبلاء ج۳ ص۱۱۱⁩ · https://shamela.ws/book/10906/2720
> *English:* "So he took it for them — and took none for himself in particular."

**⁨عبرت⁩:** The last act of a commander in a lost cause is to get his men out, and then to stand where he stood.

**Hands-up?** no

## ADVERSARIAL RE-CHECK

Pass run 2026-09-13 against the cached pages under `sources/shamela/10906/`. Every statement
blockquote was re-extracted mechanically, matched character-for-character against the page file named
by its own URL, and the printed page in the citation was compared with that file's `PRINTED PAGE:`
header. Nothing below is a stylistic change.

**What was checked.** **111** statement blockquotes (55 event cards plus the blockquotes in §§1–12,
*What could NOT be established* and *Not for the hall*), and in addition **94** inline Arabic spans in
the prose and in §0. Every URL resolves to a cached page; every book id is **10906** (⁨سیر⁩), which is
on the safe list. **No page cited anywhere in this note belongs to 9783 (⁨الطبري⁩) or to 12320
(⁨ابن خلدون⁩).** No printed page in any citation disagreed with its file's own header.

**Result after the pass:** 58 blockquotes match the page exactly; 42 differ only in that the quotation
stops at the end of its sentence where the page continues with the editor's marker and then the stop;
2 are ellipsis-joined and both halves are exact; 9 straddle a page break and are cited with a page
range. **None is unlocated. Nothing had to be deleted.**

### Quotations corrected — 16 blockquotes

The defect in eight of these was the same one: an **editor's footnote marker falling inside the quoted
sentence had been silently dropped**, which the note's own ⚠ standing warning forbids. The marker is
now restored exactly where the page puts it, spacing included.

| Where | Restored | Page |
|---|---|---|
| §4 ⁨الأشتر⁩, the `⁨وَكَانَ شَهْماً⁩…` notice | `⁨زَعِراً⁩ (١) ⁨، أَلَبَّ⁩` | ⁨ج۴ ص۳۴⁩ · 3187 |
| §4 and **E-PG20** | `⁨شَهِدَ صِفِّيْنَ⁩ (٢) ⁨مَعَ عَلِيٍّ⁩` | ⁨ج۴ ص۳۴⁩ · 3187 |
| §6 and **E-PG26** | `⁨فَقُتِلَ يَوْمَئِذٍ⁩ (٢) . ⁨وَكَانَ مَوْصُوَفاً⁩` | ⁨ج۳ ص۴۸۶⁩ · 3094 |
| §7 and **E-PG31** | `⁨نَسْتَعْدِيكَ⁩ (٣) ⁨عَلَى أُمَّتِنَا⁩` | ⁨ج۳ ص۴۶۵⁩ · 3073 |
| §12 and **E-PG52** | `⁨لَقَبِيْحٌ⁩ (٢) ⁨أَنْ أُفَارِقَ⁩` | ⁨ج۳ ص۱۱۰⁩ · 2719 |
| §12 and **E-PG53** | `⁨أَمْدَدْتُمَا⁩ (٥) ⁨عَلِيّاً⁩` | ⁨ج۳ ص۱۱۰⁩ · 2719 |
| **E-PG25** | `⁨يَصِلَ إِلَيْهَا⁩ (١) . ⁨قُلْتُ⁩` | ⁨ج۳ ص۱۰۹⁩ · 2718 |
| **E-PG32** | `⁨فَأَطْلَقَ⁩ (٤) ⁨حَبْوَتَهُ⁩` | ⁨ج۳ ص۴۶۶⁩ · 3074 |

Two further corrections of the same class, and one of a different class:

- **§9 and E-PG36 — the prophecy about ⁨جرير ؓ⁩.** The printed edition encloses the prophetic speech in
  **round brackets**, and both copies had stripped them. Restored as
  `(إِنَّهُ سَيَدْخُلُ … مِسْحَةَ مَلَكٍ)` — سیر ج۲ ص۵۳۱ · 2511.
- **E-PG49 — the only quotation that was not on its page at all.** The statement read
  `فَقَالَ حُذَيْفَةُ: يَغْفِرُ اللهُ لَكُمْ ... فَتَصَدَّقَ حُذَيْفَةُ بِدِيَتِهِ عَلَى المُسْلِمِيْنَ.`
  Neither half is in ⁨الذهبي⁩'s text on ⁨ج۲ ص۳۶۲⁩. Both stand only in the **editor's footnote (٢)**, where
  they are **unvocalised**, and they come from **two different routes** — ⁨البخاري⁩'s through ⁨عائشہ ؓ⁩ and
  ⁨ابن إسحاق⁩'s through ⁨محمود بن لبيد⁩ — spliced together with an ellipsis; and the note had then **added
  vocalisation of its own**, which is composed Arabic. The whole statement has been replaced with the
  sentence ⁨الذهبي⁩ actually writes on that page:
  `وَلَمَّا شَدُّوْا عَلَى اليَمَانِ يَوْمَئِذٍ، بَقِيَ حُذَيْفَةُ يَصِيْحُ: أَبِي! أَبِي! يَا قَوْمُ! فَرَاحَ خَطَأً، فَتَصَدَّقَ حُذَيْفَةُ عَلَيْهِم بِدِيَتِهِ (٢) .`
  The English rendering was rewritten to match it, and the card's *What happened* no longer says the
  Prophet ﷺ offered the blood-money, which is in the footnote and not in ⁨الذهبي⁩'s text.

**Nine inline Arabic spans were corrected the same way**, in §0 and §§7, 10, 11 and *Not for the hall*:
`⁨لَهُ⁩: ⁨صُحْبَةٌ، وَوِفَادَةٌ⁩` (colon restored) · `⁨اللَّهُمَّ إِنَّا نَسْتَعْدِيكَ⁩` (had been quoted straight
through a dropped marker) · `⁨زَعِراً⁩ (١) ،` · `⁨وَمَنْ لَكَ إِمَامٌ مِثْلُ عَلِيٍّ⁩` twice (the page's `!`
falls after the marker `(٦)`, so it is not part of the quoted words) · `⁨فاعتزل تلك الفرق كلها⁩` and
`⁨يغفر الله لكم⁩`, both of which stand **unvocalised** in the editor's footnotes and had been vocalised
here, i.e. composed — the second is now given in English only · `⁨وهو باطل كما في⁩ " ⁨الاستيعاب⁩ "` with
the edition's own spacing · and the ﷺ that had been substituted for the printed
`-⁨صَلَّى اللَّهُ عَلَيْهِ وَسَلَّمَ⁩-` in three quoted headings (§0 ⁨حذيفة ؓ⁩, §12 ⁨قيس ؓ⁩ twice).

**Two genealogies were replaced by the headings themselves.** §7 and §10 carried the nasab of
⁨حجر بن عدي⁩ and of ⁨الأشعث ؓ⁩ as unvocalised inline strings matching no page. They are now quoted as
⁨الذهبي⁩ heads them — ⁨سیر ج۳ ص۴۶۲⁩ · 3070 and ⁨ج۲ ص۳۷⁩ · 2017 — and the claim they support, that the two
lines meet at ⁨جبلة بن عدي بن ربيعة⁩, is true of the headings exactly as printed.

### Page-citation fixes — 2

- **§7, the ⁨عائشہ ؓ⁩ / ⁨معاوية ؓ⁩ exchange** (`⁨أَقَتَلْتَ حُجْراً؟⁩`) was cited **⁨ج۳ ص۴۶۷⁩** alone. The word
  `⁨أَقَتَلْتَ⁩` is the **last word of ⁨ص۴۶۶⁩** (index 3074); only `⁨حُجْراً؟⁩` onward is on ⁨ص۴۶۷⁩. The citation
  is now **⁨ج۳ ص۴۶۶⁩–۴۶۷** and carries a flag saying where the break falls.
- The matching bullet in **§0** was corrected to the same range.

### A printed-edition reading flagged, not corrected — 1

- **E-PG6.** The heading on ⁨ج۲ ص۳۸۰⁩–۳۸۱ reads `⁨أَبُو مُوْسَى الأَشْعَرِيُّ، التَّمِيْمِيُّ، الفَقِيْهُ،⁩
  ⁨المُقْرِئُ⁩` — ⁨التميمي⁩ where one would expect ⁨الأشعري⁩'s own nisba. The English had quietly dropped the
  word. The Arabic is kept exactly as the page has it, the word is now rendered, and a one-line flag
  beneath the block says so.

Ids, field order and tier values were re-checked mechanically: **E-PG1–E-PG55, unique and sequential,
every card carrying Tier · When (with a certainty label) · Map · What happened · a statement block ·

### Guardrail and register fixes — 3

- **E-PG50's ⁨عبرت⁩** read that ⁨قيس ؓ⁩'s career "is the most direct answer the history itself gives to what
  ⁨سقيفہ⁩ was about." That is this note delivering a verdict on a contested event between Companions.
  Rewritten to state the fact and stop.
- **E-PG54's *What happened*** had ⁨قيس ؓ⁩ "counted among the five shrewdest heads of the fitna, **and he
  knew it**" — an interior state no source reports. Removed, and the count attributed to ⁨الزهري⁩.
- **E-PG49's *What happened*** had the Prophet ﷺ wanting to pay the blood-money; that is in the
  editor's footnote, through ⁨ابن إسحاق⁩, and not in ⁨الذهبي⁩'s text. Removed.

Scanned and clean: **cast / character / villain / hero / episode / trailer / plot twist** appear
nowhere; no ⁨لقب⁩ is invented for any Companion; honorifics are present and correct; no sentence decides
between Companions; there is no "in fact", "the truth is" or "he was wrong to" anywhere in the file.
Where a page carries a refusal or a grading, the note already has it — ⁨الذهبي⁩'s
`مَا أَدْرِي مَا وَجْهُ هَذَا القَوْلِ` (ج۲ ص۳۹۴), `هَذَا مُنْقَطِعٌ` (ج۳ ص۱۱۱), `وَهَذَا خَطَأٌ`
(⁨ج۳ ص۷۷⁩), and the editor's `⁨فإن صح هذا عن حذيفة ولا إخاله يصح⁩` (⁨ج۲ ص۳۹۴⁩),
`⁨وهو باطل كما في⁩ " ⁨الاستيعاب⁩ "` (⁨ج۳ ص۱۱۲⁩) and `⁨فالخبر لا يصح⁩` (⁨ج۳ ص۵۷⁩). Re-reading the ⁨حجر⁩ and
⁨الأشعث⁩ pages (3070–3075 and 2017–2022) turned up no further ⁨الذهبي⁩ verdict that the note had dropped.

### Still unresolved

- **Inline Arabic inside English sentences.** About ninety quoted Arabic spans still sit inside English
  sentences in §0 and in the prose, where `DECISIONS.md` #25 wants a blockquote of its own with page
  and URL beneath. This pass **verified every one of them character-for-character** and corrected the
  nine that were wrong, but did not restructure them; §0 is a licence list and would roughly treble in
  length. Flagged for a later formatting pass, not a factual one.
- **Terminal footnote markers.** 42 statement blocks end their sentence where the page continues with
  the editor's marker and then the stop. The marker falls *outside* the quoted sentence, so it was left
  off — a convention, not a verified rule, recorded here so the next reader does not re-derive it.
- **Cross-page quotations.** Nine blocks straddle a page break and are cited with a range
  (⁨ج۳ ص۲۲⁩–۲۳ · ⁨ج۳ ص۵۲۸⁩–۵۲۹ · ⁨ج۳ ص۱۰۷⁩–۱۰۸ · ⁨ج۲ ص۳۸۰⁩–۳۸۱ · ⁨ج۳ ص۴۶۴⁩–۴۶۵ · ⁨ج۳ ص۴۶۶⁩–۴۶۷). Each URL points at
  the page carrying the larger part; the other half was confirmed on the adjacent cached index.
- **The open questions of the note itself are unchanged** and were re-confirmed against the pages:
  ⁨الأشتر⁩'s death year, ⁨صعصعة بن صوحان⁩'s death year and tribe, ⁨قيس بن سعد ؓ⁩'s death year, whether
  ⁨هاشم بن عتبة⁩ is a Companion, the thirteen-against-twenty arithmetic at ⁨عذراء⁩, and who killed
  ⁨محمد بن أبي بكر ؓ⁩. Nothing in the cached pages settles any of them.
